1.    Please provide an executive summary describing the nature and scope of the proposal. Additionally,
please include the following: (1) identification of all parties, (2) description of the applicant and it’s
licensure track record, (3) the type of transaction proposed including description of the transaction and
relevant costs, (4) summary of all transfer documents, (5) summary of the organizational structure of the
applicant and its affiliates, and (6) whether the facility will be accredited.

Lincolnwood Operator LLC d/b/a/ Lincolnwood Rehabilitation and Healthcare Center
(“Lincolnwood” or “Applicant”) is the current operator of the licensed skilled nursing facility known
as Lincolnwood Rehabilitation Center located at 610 Smithfield Road in North Providence, Rhode
Island (the “Facility”) since 2020. Lincolnwood is a licensed 210-bed facility and offers short-term
rehabilitation services and long-term care, Alzheimer’s and dementia care, hospice and respite services.
Lincolnwood is planning to restructure its ultimate owners for estate planning purposes (the
“Reorganization”).
There will be no change to the Applicant who is the current licensee of the Facility as a result of the
Restructuring. The current licensee will remain the licensee and continue to provide the same quality
services to its residents.


The Parties

Lincolnwood is currently a subsidiary of Quinto Delta LLC (“Quinto Delta”) (88.6%), UKR
Consulting LLC (“UKR”) (10%) and Skilled Venture LLC (1.4%). Quinto Delta is currently a
subsidiary of Tryko Delta Holdings LLC (“Tryko Delta”) (75.4%) and Norman Rokeach. The
members of Tryko Delta are the YK 2013 Delta Trust, SK 2013 Delta Trust and YR 2013 Delta Trust.
The members of UKR are currently Uri Kahanow and Aviva Kahanow.

Following the Reorganization of the membership entities, the members of Lincolnwood will be Quinto
Nexgen LLC (“Quinto”) (78.6%), Skilled Venture LLC (1.4%) and UKR Nexgen LLC (“UKR”)
(20%). The members of Quinto will be Tryko Nexgen Holdings LLC (“Tryko”) (72.26%) and
RSBRMK Holdings LLC (“RSBRMK”) (27.74%). The members of Tryko will be YR Nexgen Trust
(50.002%), SK Nexgen Trust (24.999%) and YK Nexgen Trust (24.999%). The members of
RSBRMK will be NFR 2020 Irrevocable Trust (99.999%) and Fraide Rokeach (.001%). The
members of UKR will be UAK 2020 Irrevocable Trust (99.999%) and Uri Kahanow (.001%). The
pre and post Reorganizational charts are at Tab 15.

The Transaction

Pursuant to the September 3, 2021 Assignment of Membership Interest in Lincolnwood Operator
LLC (attached at Tab 14), Quinto Delta will assign its membership interest in Lincolnwood to Quinto.
The purchase price for the assignment will be the market value of the membership interest as
determined by an appraiser prior to the Closing.

Applicant’s Long-Term Care Operations Experience.

The Applicant and its affiliates have a proven history of providing high-quality long-term care
services to their nursing facility residents. Significantly, the Applicant has operated Lincolnwood
since acquiring it out of receivership in 2020 and has provided high quality services to the residents




                                                    1

===== PAGE 5 =====
and community. The nursing facility affiliates of the Applicant are third-generation operators and
own thirty-five (39) skilled nursing facilities in seven (7) states: Maryland, Massachusetts, New
Jersey, Rhode Island, Pennsylvania and Virginia. The Applicant is currently the licensed operator
of Lincolnwood Rehabilitation and Healthcare Center, and its affiliate acquired Elmhurst
Rehabilitation and Healthcare Center in 2016 in Providence and Bayview Operator, LLC in North
Kingstown, Rhode Island in July 2021. Several of the Applicant’s affiliated skilled nursing facilities
have received four and five-star ratings in U.S. New and World Reports “Best Nursing Homes” list.

The Applicant is independently operated and managed by an experienced facility-employed
administrator. It maintains consulting agreements with Marquis Health Consulting Services
(“Marquis”) for assistance with administrative services such as policy and procedure development
and marking services; with Clinical Care Consulting (“Clinical Care”) for assistance with clinical
planning and strategy; and with Cedarbridge Financial Services (“Cedarbridge”) for assistance
with back office, billing, payroll and account receivable services. Neither Marquis, Clinical Care
nor Cedarbridge serve in any capacity as operator or manager of Lincolnwood. They also do not
have any authority to hire or fire the Facility’s chief executive officer, maintain or control the books
and records of the Facility, dispose of assets or incur liabilities on behalf of the Facility, or adopt or
enforce policies regarding the operation of the Facility. Please see consulting agreements attached
at Tab 1.

All the facilities associated with the Applicant are in substantial compliance with the licensing
agencies in their respective states

11. Please provide a concise description of the services currently offered by the licensed entity and identify
any services that will be added, terminated, expanded, or reduced and state the reasons therefore:

The existing facility currently provides short-term rehabilitation services and long-term care. In
addition, the existing facility makes available hospice and respite services through service agreements
with licensed hospice providers as detailed in the response to Question 1 above. The Applicant will
continue all services at Lincolnwood following the reorganization.

12. Please identify the long-term plans of the applicant with respect to the health care programs and health
care services to be provided at the facility:

The Applicant will continue the quality services currently provided at the Facility. The Applicant will
also continue making any needed upgrades to the physical plant.

16. If the proposed owner, operator or director owned, operated or directed a health care facility (both
within and outside Rhode Island) within the past three years, please demonstrate the record of that person(s)
with respect to access of traditionally underserved populations to its health care facilities.

It is the policy of all the Applicant and its affiliates not to discriminate because of race, creed, color,
religion, national origin, sex, gender, handicap, disability, blindness, source of sponsorship, source of
payment, marital status, age, sexual preference, genetic disposition, or carrier status in the
admission, retention and care of residents and patients. All persons and organizations that have
occasion to either refer prospective residents or patients to Lincolnwood Center are advised to do so
without regard to the person’s race, creed, color, religion, national origin, sex, gender, handicap,
disability, blindness, source of sponsorship, source of payment, marital status, age, sexual
preference, genetic disposition, or carrier status. The Applicant and its affiliates have a proven track
record of providing services to the traditionally underserved population

17. Please identify the proposed immediate and long-term plans of the applicant to ensure adequate and
appropriate access to the program and health care services to be provided by the health care facility/ies to
traditionally underserved populations.

The Applicant’s immediate plan is to ensure that access to services is maintained for all existing
patients without interruption or dislocation. In addition, as noted in the response to Question 16,
Applicant is committed to meeting the physical, emotional and psychosocial needs of all patients,
regardless of race, religion, socioeconomic status or payer source and will continue to provide services
without discrimination to the residents of Lincolnwood including the underserved population.

21. Please identify any planned actions of the applicant to reduce, limit, or contain health care costs and
improve the efficiency with which health care services are delivered to the citizens of this state.

The Applicant will continue to maintain clinically guided programs to reduce hospitalizations or
unplanned discharges by working closely with physicians and acute care partners. The Applicant
will continue to employ specialized electronic medical records, as well as specialty clinical
programs, along with telehealth to help reduce healthcare costs. In addition, the Applicant will
continue to use UrgentSNF for patients. This provides an alternative option to individuals who do
not meet acute admissions criteria but require and/or will benefit from supervisory skilled nursing
oversight or stabilization. These services are provided at a discounted rate for up to 14 days. This
rate is all inclusive of room/board, meals, therapy and nursing services.

22. Please provide a copy of the Quality Assurance Policies (for the services) and a detailed explanation
of how quality assurance for patient services will be implemented at the facility/ies by the applicant.

The Applicant will maintain a robust Quality Assurance program. See Tab 22 for the Quality
Assurance Policies. As set forth therein the Quality Assurance program will ensure facility systems
and processes to support the delivery of quality care and services. It likewise will help to identify
actual and potential negative outcomes relative to resident care and resolve them appropriately.

===== PAGE 9 =====
      A. The immediate and long-term financial feasibility of the proposed financing plan;

         There is no proposed financing for this Transaction.

      B. The relative availability of funds for capital and operating needs; and

         The Applicant has successfully operated the facility since March 2021. There will be no change
         to its operations or the availability of funds for capital and operating needs as a result of this
         Reorganization.

   C. The applicant’s financial capability.

         The Applicant has successfully operated the facility since March 2021. As set forth in the
         financial statements included at Tab 28, the Applicant and its owner have access to sufficient
         funds for all operating needs, reflecting its financial capability to operate the facility.

          The Applicant specifically shares common ownership with Tryko Delta Holdings, LLC and
          Quinto Delta, LLC (its previous owners). As such, the Applicant affiliate’s financial capacity
          is documented in the CPA letter (Tab 23). Quinto Delta acquired over six nursing home
          facilities over the last 18 months, including the Applicant. Quinto Delta’s asset portfolio
          includes both the operator and real estate components of the five nursing facilities; the
          company is diversified not only across the facilities in terms of market locations, but also
          across operations and real property. The Quinto Delta income statement itemizes the patient
          mix across the five facilities and reflects the annualized aggregate operating revenue and
          expense of the facilities. Quinto Delta’s balance sheet indicates a debt to equity ratio of 1.0,
          showing unusual financial strength for a company that has undertaken five acquisitions in the
          last year.

                                         Appendix C (CONT.)

5.      Based on the format below, please provide a summary of the applicant’s administrative and operational
policies and procedures to provide individualized and resident-centered care, services, and accommodations, and a
sense of peace, safety, and community, and clearly identify how the proposal would advance these areas:

           a. Resident’s physical environment:

                   i. Accommodations for privacy vs. congregate and common areas;

                      Residents’ bedrooms allow for privacy. Private, single-occupancy rooms as
                      well as semi-private rooms provide space for private living. A privacy curtain
                      and individual closet and storage space allow for privacy in semi-private
                      rooms. Common area day rooms and dining rooms as well as a chapel allow
                      for communal activities and resident gatherings. Both private and common
                      areas are used for resident visits. Resident-directed activities and space
                      utilization is guided by the Resident’s Council.

                  ii. Choice and autonomy in personal space, fixtures, furniture;

                      Residents may furnish their room with their own furniture to the extent space
                      and fire safety allows.

                 iii. Access to and involvement in decentralized services, such as, community kitchen(s),
                      laundry, activities;

                      Each resident neighborhood has a kitchenette for resident and family use.

                 iv. Access to outdoors and outdoor activities (e.g., sunrooms, patios, gardens and
                     gardening);

                      Residents may enjoy being outdoors independently and in organized activities.
                      A shaded area is available for this purpose at Lincolnwood Center.

           b. Resident-centered systems of care:

                   i. Security systems and care delivery systems to foster autonomy, choice, and
                      negotiated risk;

                      The facility has a security system preventing those at risk for wandering from
                      exiting the facility without staff knowledge. This provides both for the safety
                      of residents and for autonomy of residents who are safe to exit. A CCTV
                      system is installed in common areas and entrances and exits to allow for
                      observation for safety and security purposes without limiting movement. A
                      resident call system alerts staff via pagers when residents call for assistance.




                                                   20

===== PAGE 24 =====
          This provides an immediate notification to the resident’s caregiver of the need
          for help.

      ii. Individualized daily/nightly scheduling (e.g., daily rhythm, going to bed, waking);

          Residents are interviewed to identify their preferences which are incorporated
          into the plan of care. Care preferences include sleep and wake times,
          bath/shower schedules, activity preferences, etc. Should a resident be unable
          to provide information regarding individualized preferences, family members
          and resident representatives are queried for the information. This
          information is included in the resident care plan, communicated to the entire
          team, reviewed with each care plan meeting, and updated accordingly.

      iii. Dining flexibility (e.g., time, access to dining style and menu choice);

          Daily menus include regular and alternate meals along with always available
          choices. Residents are queried for likes/dislikes regarding food preferences.
          Family members and resident representatives are involved for residents
          unable to respond. Residents may choose an in-room or dining room setting,
          and this preference of setting may vary with each meal. Residents are
          encouraged to participate in food council to determine seasonal menu choices
          and always available options. Dining room meal services include restaurant-
          style services. Information regarding meal preferences is incorporated into
          the care plans and reviewed with each care plan meeting and updated
          accordingly.

      iv. Lifestyle/activities flexibility;

          Activities are resident-centered with an array of events and opportunities
          available at any given time for resident engagement.

c. Workforce administration:

       i. How do staffing schedules and assignments ensure consistent delivery of resident
          services and foster relationship building?

          Lincolnwood Center supports the Consistent Assignment model of care.
          Consistent Assignment allows for relationships to form between resident and
          caregiver and for the rhythms of life to be learned. The Interact Stop and
          Watch program is most effective when staffing with Consistent Assignment
          and fall prevention, observation of weight loss, and maintenance of skin
          integrity are all more effective with Consistent Assignment. Resident Life
          Stories tell the life story of the resident to caregivers allowing for more
          meaningful engagement between residents and caregivers and the support of a
          more meaningful and engaging activities program for each resident.




                                          21

===== PAGE 25 =====
ii. Administrative status strategies for dealing with licensed staff turn-over (e.g.
    Registered nurses, Licenses Practical nurses, Nursing Assistants)

   A robust employee retention program and a culture of recognition and respect
   is the key to minimizing turnover and thereby maintaining consistent care
   standards. Lincolnwood Center maintain such a program. The longevity of
   the staff at the current facility is one of its strengths and a feature which
   Lincolnwood Center hopes to maintain in the future.

                         CONSULTING SERVICES AGREEMENT

        THIS CONSULTING SERVICES AGREEMENT (this "Agreement"), dated as of
____________ 8
 March          ____, 2021 is by and between LINCOLNWOOD OPERATOR LLC, a Rhode
Island limited liability company ( “Operator”) licensed to operate a nursing home in
Rhode Island known as Lincolnwood Rehabilitation and Healthcare Center ("Facility")
with an address of 610 Smithfield Road, North Providence, Rhode Island 02904 and CLINICAL
CARE CONSULTANTS, LLC a New Jersey limited liability company, having its
principal place of business at 1608 Route 88, Suite 301, Brick, NJ 08724 ("Consultant").
Operator and Consultant may each be referred to as a "Party" and collectively as the
"Parties".

       WHEREAS, Consultant provides limited consulting services for long term
care facilities in areas which require the understanding of clinical function;

        WHEREAS, Consultant does not own and is not an operator or nursing home
administrator or a Management Company and does not itself provide or direct clinical
services;

        WHEREAS, Operator is and shall remain legally responsible for the operation
of the Facility and while maintaining responsibility and control;

       WHEREAS, Operator desires to retain Consultant and Consultant desires to provide
limited services as set forth and pursuant to the terms and conditions set forth herein;

       NOW THEREFORE, in consideration of the mutual covenants and undertakings of
the Parties hereto, and for other good and valuable consideration, the receipt and sufficiency
of which are hereby acknowledged, the Parties hereto, intending to be legally bound, do
hereby agree as follows:

                                   ARTICLE 1
                            RELATIONSHIP OF THE PARTIES

1.     Retention of Authority by Operator.

         1.1 Appointment of Consultant. Operator hereby engages Consultant to
 provide certain clinical consulting services (the "Services"), as described in Article 2.
 Consultant accepts such appointment, pursuant to the terms and conditions contained
 herein.

         1.2 Third Party Beneficiaries. By virtue of entering into this Agreement or providing
 any services hereunder, Consultant does not create a duty to any third-parties, including but
 not limited to residents, and there are no intended third-party beneficiaries to this Agreement.
 Consultant’s only duty is to the Operator and the extent of that duty is fully set forth and
 limited by the terms of this Agreement.

        1.3 Compliance with Law. Without regard to any advice or directions from the
 Consultant, Operator at all times retains the obligation to act ethically and to provide all
 goods and services and to invoice and establish and implement policies and procedures
 which are consistent with all applicable Federal, State and local statutes, rules and

===== PAGE 33 =====
regulations, including without limiting the generality of the foregoing, anti-kickback
statutes, false claims acts, reverse false claims acts, HIPPA and other privacy statutes;
Stark and other anti-referral legislation; the FLMA and other labor laws and employee
and/or resident safety regulations and all other rules and laws. Neither the Consultant
nor the Operator desires for the Facility to act in any way contrary to its responsibilities
and notwithstanding anything contained herein to the contrary, the Operator is solely
responsible for ensuring that all of its actions are consistent with all rules, regulations,
laws and all required standards of conduct.

         1.4 Communications. The Operator understands that communication from the
Consultant may sometimes be inartful, subject to misinterpretation, or not fully reflect the
views of its author or the Consultants. In addition, for efficiency and to reduce the costs
of the services provided hereunder, the Consultant does not intend to utilize legal Counsel
to screen communication and instead relies upon the Facility to do so. To the extent that
anything written or spoken by Consultant or anyone on its behalf, could be interpreted as
illegal, immoral or improper, such communication must be discarded to the extent it is
unethical, legally impermissible and/or to the extent the same is inconsistent with best
practices. The Facility understands this obligation and accept the same.

        1.5 Retention of Ownership and Control. Operator holds and shall continue to
hold all necessary licenses and permits to operate the Facility. The Nursing Home
Administrator (the “Administrator”) is licensed to and shall manage the facility operations.
Notwithstanding anything contained herein to the contrary, in no way does the Operator,
the Licensed Nursing Home Administrator or any of the Department Heads herein cede its,
or collectively their, responsibility for the operation to the Consultant nor does the
Consultant accept such responsibility. Operator shall remain responsible for ensuring that
any services provided pursuant to this Agreement complies with all pertinent provisions of
Federal, State, and local laws, rules, regulations and ordinances, and standards of
accreditation. Operator shall retain all ultimate authority for the overall policies,
procedures, operation and assets of the Facility. All final decisions and all responsibility
for ensuring the existence and implementation of adequate and appropriate policies and
procedures and all responsibility for caring for residents in addition to responsibility for
properly assessing, coding, documenting and all other aspects of providing and billing for
Facility services shall be the sole responsibility of the Operator. It is expressly understood
that Consultant’s role is merely suggestive and advisory and without liability. Operator
shall not delegate to Consultant, and Consultant will not be responsible for, any powers
and/or responsibilities not specifically allocated to Consultant in this Agreement. Without
limiting the generality of the foregoing, Operator retains and shall not delegate authority
and responsibility over the following:

===== PAGE 34 =====
                 1.2.1 Patient Care;

                1.2.2        Hiring, terminating and setting of all rules relating to any of the
 Facility's employees, including, but not limited to: key management employees, the Administrator,
 the Medical Director, the Director of Nursing and members of the medical staff of the Facility;

                 1.2.3 The books and records of the Facility and/or Operator and all financial issues;

                 1.2.4 Disposing of assets and incurring liabilities on behalf of the Operator and/or
 the Facility;

                1.2.5 Adopting, approving and enforcing policies and procedures regarding the
 operation of the Facility and/or the care of residents;

                 1.2.6 Setting or modifying the operating and/or capital and/or other budgets
 relating to the Facility;

                 1.2.7 Billing and/or collection;

                 1.2.8 Compliance with rules, laws and regulations;

                 1.2.9 Day-to-day operations; and

                 1.2.10 Any other task which is not expressly delegated to the Consultant pursuant
 hereto.

        1.3    Status of Parties. Consultant is      a non-exclusive independent contractor that
engages in the operation of its own business. Neither Party is, nor shall be considered, the agent or
employee of the other Party for any purpose. Neither Party has the authority to enter contracts or
assume any obligations for the other Party, nor to make any warranties or representations on behalf
of the other Party. Nothing in this Agreement is to be construed as establishing a relationship of co-
partners and/or joint venturers between the Parties. The Parties understand and agree that they are
not co-employers and no Party will withhold on behalf of any employee or agent of the other Party
any sums for income tax, unemployment insurance, social security, or any other withholding
pursuant to any law or requirement of any governmental body relating to the payment of employee
benefits.

        1.4 Eligibility of Consultant. Neither Consultant nor its principals and/or its employees are
precluded from providing the services as described herein or are subject to disciplinary action by any
licensing board including without limitation suspension or revocation of a license. On a go forward
basis, consultant shall perform no less frequent than monthly, LEIE checks to determine it employee
and principal’s eligibility and shall immediately notify the Operator in the event of any change in
circumstances. Consultant shall be responsible to Operator for any fines and/or other penalties
imposed upon it in relation to the Consultant’s breach of this Article 1.4.

===== PAGE 35 =====
                  CONSULTING AND ADMINISTRATIVE SERVICES

2.     Scope of Services. Consultant shall provide the following services:

          2.1 Assist the Operator customize its policies and procedures. It is
 understood that the Operator utilizes and intends to continue to utilize policies and
 procedures which are purchased by it from a reputable third party. Operator is and
 shall remain responsible for the adequacy of all policies and procedures and the
 appropriate implementation and monitoring of the same.
          2.2 Assist the QAPI Committee. The Consultant shall assist the QAPI
 committee as needed in its confidential and protected efforts to identify how the
 Facility organization is performing, including assessing clinical data to help identify
 where and why Facility performance is at risk or has failed to meet standards and
 identifying areas of opportunity and testing new approaches to fix underlying causes
 of persistent/systemic problems or barriers to improvement and continuously study
 and improve processes with the intent to better services or outcomes and assist the
 QAPI Committee to improve processes involved in healthcare delivery and resident
 quality of life. It is understood that by virtue of Consultant’s support or involvement,
 it is not responsible for the effectiveness of the QAPI Committee, which shall remain
 the sole responsibility of the Operator. Unless otherwise required by law, Consultant
 shall keep all matters discussed during the QAPI process confidential—except as to
 other member of the QAPI Committee or those assisting the QAPI Committee. To
 the extent asked to disclose the same, Consultant shall promptly advise the Operator
 who, at Operator’s expense, shall be given the opportunity to assert any applicable
 privilege, such as the quality assurance privilege.

         2.3 Periodically observe and report observations concerning the Dietary,
 Physical Therapy, Social Services and Nursing Departments to the QAPI Committee
 or representative thereof such as the Administrator or the applicable Department Head
 to report to and be addressed by the QAPI Committee. Such observation may include
 sitting in on any Facility meetings during which clinical matter are or may be
 discussed. It is expressly understood that Consultant is not the manager of any of the
 foregoing departments and it is not anticipated that the Consultant will observe most
 issues, potential issues, or opportunities for improvement. Its job is only to offer
 advice as it becomes aware of issues. Consultant shall not be held responsible for
 failing to identify any of the foregoing and or failure to implement any
 recommendation.




                                               3

===== PAGE 36 =====
         2.4 Assist the Facility in connection with preparedness for governmental
 surveys, inspections and/or investigations and responses to any adverse findings
 with regard to the same. Without limiting the generality of the foregoing, the
 Consultant may act as a liaison to the governmental representative(s), assisting
 the Facility in its attempts to cooperate with governmental officials and ensuring
 the information provided is complete and accurate, drafting of plans of
 corrections, assessing dates of compliance, preparing IDRs or IIDRs and the
 formation of post survey, inspection, or investigation recommendations, and
 working to support legal or other consultants. All work performed in
 anticipation of governmental audit, investigation or litigation of any sort, and all
 communication to or from legal counsel or concerning advice from counsel shall
 be held in strict confidence and shall not be disclosed to third parties. Should
 anyone seek such confidential information, Consultant shall immediately notify
 the Operator, so the Operator may at its expense, protect that which is subject to
 the Work Product Doctrine, the Attorney Client Privilege or is otherwise
 privileged.

         2.5 As requested, assist the Facility and/or its consultants, lawyers and/or
 insurers in performing risk management assessments and analysis. As these functions are
 typically in anticipation of litigation or to support defense of existing litigation, such work
 is to be held as confidential. Should anyone seek such confidential information, Consultant
 shall immediately notify the Operator, so the Operator may at its expense, protect that which is
 subject to the Work Product Doctrine, the Attorney Client Privilege or is otherwise privileged.

         2.6 Assist the Facility to develop controls and systems for honestly, ethically and
 appropriately assessing and recording resident’s condition and needs as the foregoing
 relates to Minimum Data Set (MDS) assessment data and if applicable, Case Mix Index.
 Notwithstanding the foregoing, Consultant shall not be responsible for accuracy of billing
 and the full responsibility for the same shall remain with the Operator.

         2.7 Make yourselves generally available to Facility Leadership and consultants as an
 educated and experienced resource to offer opinions, without warranty, on clinical and
 operational issues as they relate to the Facility.




                                         ARTICLE 3
                                       COMPENSATION
3.     Compensation

      3.1     Fees. In consideration for the Services provided by Consultant during the
Term of this Agreement, Operator shall pay to Consultant fees in an amount of $22,800.00
                                                4

===== PAGE 37 =====
("Service Fee") per month, on the first day of each month following execution. Should the
cost of services exceed the fee, Consultant shall inform the Operator and Operator may at
its discretion pay the greater of the actual cost from the date of notice forward or cancel
such services.

        3.2    The Parties agree that the compensation provided herein is consistent with
fair market value in an arm's-length transaction and is not and has not been determined in a
manner that takes into account the volume or value of any referrals or business otherwise
generated for or with respect to the Facility or between the Parties or any of the respective
owners thereof for which payment may be made in whole or in part under Medicare or any
state health care program or under any other payor program.

                                       ARTICLE 4
                                       TERM AND
                                      TERMINATION

4.     Term and Termination

       4.1     Term. The term of this Agreement will begin as of the date hereof (the
"Commencement Date") and will continue for an initial term of one (1) year (the "Term").
Thereafter, this Agreement will renew automatically for one (1) year periods (each, a
"Renewal Term") unless sooner terminated in accordance with the terms and conditions of
this Agreement.

       4.2     Termination. This Agreement may be terminated upon the occurrence of
any of the following events:

                4.2.1 Termination for Cause. This Agreement may be terminated by either
Party for cause. "Cause" shall mean a material breach of any provision, obligation, covenant,
representation or warranty set forth in this Agreement, which breach or violation is not cured
to the commercially reasonable satisfaction of the non-breaching Party within thirty (30) days
of its giving of detailed written notice of such material breach to the other Party.
Notwithstanding the foregoing, either Party may terminate this Agreement immediately if
the breaching Party does not, within seven (7) days, cure a failure by it to comply with a
material pertinent provision of any Federal, State or local statute, rule or regulation in
carrying out its responsibilities hereunder.

             4.2.2 Immediate Termination. This Agreement may be terminated
immediately by either Party in writing upon the following: disbarment of either Party from
the Medicare or Medicaid programs and, in the case of Consultant, disbarment of any of its
owners. Consultant shall immediately notify Operator in writing in the event any of its
employees that provide services to the Facility pursuant to this Agreement are excluded
from the Medicare and/or Medicaid programs.

                                              5

===== PAGE 38 =====
                4.2.3 Without Cause. Each party may terminate this Agreement without
cause upon thirty (30) days prior written notice to the other party.
                4.2.4 Post Termination Actions. Upon termination, all work performed by
Consultant and all of its notes, data, information gathered, correspondence, calculations, and
material otherwise received, utilized or generated in the performance of the duties set forth
herein, shall be deemed the property of the Operator and shall be promptly tendered by the
Consultant to the Operator. Consultants shall not retain a copy of the foregoing. Without
limiting the generality of the foregoing, the Consultant shall return all work performed for the
Operator, pursuant to this Agreement and in its possession as a result of this Agreement or its
relationship with the Operator. The Consultant shall also refrain for one year after termination
from directly or indirectly hiring or soliciting on its own accord any for any other person,
anyone who worked for the Operator at the time of termination or within 6 months of such
time.


                                          ARTICLE 5
                                          COVENANTS

5.     Nondisclosure of Proprietary Information; Nonsolicitation; .

        5.1    The Parties acknowledge and agree that during the Term they will have
access to Confidential Information (as defined below) and other proprietary information
of the Parties as well as personal healthcare information of Residents and information
which is protected by one or more privileges such as the work product doctrine, the
attorney client privilege and or the quality assurance/peer review privilege. Neither
Party shall, nor shall its members, employees, representatives and agents, except as may
be required under applicable law or by any lawful subpoena, court order or legal
process, at any time without the other Party's prior written consent (i) disclose any such
information to any third party, or (ii) reproduce or utilize any such information in
furtherance of any other business venture. If a Party is required under applicable law or
by lawful subpoena, court order, or legal process to disclose any Confidential Information
or other proprietary information of the other, such disclosing party shall provide sufficient
notice thereof to the other to enable such party to seek a protective order or other appropriate
legal or equitable remedy to prevent such disclosure; provided that each party shall be
permitted to disclose the Confidential Information to its legal and other advisers.
Furthermore, Consultant shall also keep medical records of the Facility confidential in
accordance with all applicable state and Federal laws, including, but not limited to, Health
Insurance Portability and Accountability Act of 1996 ("HIPAA") and its related regulations
and shall execute and abide by the Business Associate Agreement, annexed hereto and
incorporated herein as Exhibit "A". For purposes of this Article 5, Section 5.1, the term
"Confidential Information" shall mean the non-public information of a Party, or any entity
with which a Party contracts to provide any of the Services, including, but not limited to,
formulae, patterns, compilations, programs, devices, methods, systems, techniques,
processes, financial information, business strategies, costing data, patient lists, payor lists,
                                                6

===== PAGE 39 =====
patient surveys, manuals, policies and procedures, forms, and contractual arrangements and
shall also include all potentially privileged information including that which is potentially
subject to the Work Product, Attorney Client, the QA or other privilege . Confidential
Information shall not include information which: (w) is known to the receiving Party prior to
receiving it from the other Party (except to the extent privileged); (x) is generally known to
the public; (y) is disclosed to one Party at any time by a third party who had the legal right to
disclose it; or (z) is independently developed by the other Party in compliance with law. The
provisions of this Article 5, Section 5.1 shall survive the termination or assignment of this
Agreement.
         5.2 Consultant shall not during the term of the Agreement or thereafter for a period of
12 months solicit any person who was employed by the Operator to work with or for the
consultant or hire such person directly or indirectly through another without regard to job title or
salary. It is agreed that this provision may be enforced by injunction. Consultant hereby
consents to the entry of an injunction and waives any, and all, requirements for Operator to post a
bond. Likewise, in lieu of actual damages, liquidated damages shall be imposed for all breaches
of this provision and all attorney fees and all cost of litigation relating to any injunction or
damages shall be recoverable. Liquidated damages shall be equal to one year salary for such
individual as paid by Operator, including all benefits, bonusses and the like using the annualized
rate from the last pay increase.

shall be in amounts not less than One Million Dollars ($1,000,000.00) per occurrence.
Workers' compensation and employer's liability coverage shall be in statutory amounts and
in compliance with the laws and regulations.

insurance required hereunder shall state that each Party shall be provided with thirty (30)
days notice of changes in carriers or material terms of the coverage. Each Party shall also

hereunder for a period of not less than three (3) years following the termination of this
Agreement that is underwritten on a claims made rather than on an occurrence basis, unless

                         CONSULTING SERVICES AGREEMENT

      THIS SERVICES AGREEMENT (this “Agreement”) is made and entered into as of
_________ 8
March     ___, 2021 (the “Effective Date”) by and between LINCOLNWOOD OPERATOR
LLC (“Licensee”), as licensed operator, and CEDARBRIDGE FINANCIAL SERVICES LLC
(“Cedarbridge Service Consultant”).

                                           RECITALS

      WHEREAS, Licensee is the operator of a healthcare and rehabilitation facility located at
610 Smithfield Road, North Providence, RI 02904, to be known as Lincolnwood Rehabilitation
and Healthcare Center (the “Facility”).

        WHEREAS, Cedarbridge Service Consultant is engaged in the business of providing
financial services to long term care facilities with Licensee being solely responsible for the
operation of the Facility in compliance with applicable law;

        WHEREAS, Cedarbridge Service Consultant has agreed to provide Services (as defined
in Section 2 hereof) to Licensee upon the terms and subject to the conditions hereinafter set forth;
with Licensee being solely responsible for the operation of the Facility in compliance with
applicable law and

        WHEREAS, Licensee and Cedarbridge Service Consultant have agreed to enter into this
Agreement and Licensee hereby retains Cedarbridge Service Consultant on and subject to the
terms, conditions and provisions set forth herein.

                                         AGREEMENT

       NOW THEREFORE, for and in consideration of the mutual covenants, agreements and
promises, which the parties set forth below, and other good and valuable consideration, the receipt
and sufficiency of which are hereby acknowledged, the parties agree as follows:

1.     Term. The term of this Agreement (“Term”) shall commence when the Licensee begins
       operating at the Facility and shall continue until either party sends prior written notice to
       the other party of that party’s intent to terminate this Agreement sixty (60) days prior to
       the annual anniversary of the Effective Date unless the Term is sooner terminated pursuant
       to the terms of the Agreement.

2.     Duties. Cedarbridge Service Consultant shall perform the following services (the
       “Services”):

       (a)     Provide, in coordination with Licensee’s office staff, bookkeeping, accounting and
               reporting services for the Facility pertaining to all of its accounts receivable
               (including without limitation all private pay residents, Medicare residents, and
               Medicaid residents) and accounts payable, including but not limited to, routine
               billing of its accounts and assisting in the regular or periodic performance of the
               Facility’s cash flow analysis;

===== PAGE 49 =====
(b)   When necessary, at Licensee’s cost, secure the services of additional bookkeepers
      and contract accountants to prepare and review the Facility’s financial records and
      consult with and advise the Licensee and its agents regarding their timely
      preparation, content and submittals;

(c)   Assist with and provide for the timely preparing and filing of any and all financial
      records and/or reports (including but not limited to Medicaid and/or Medicare cost
      reports) relating to the Facility’s operation, and also financial plans to be reviewed
      and approved by Licensee on a periodic basis, including but not limited to: (a) an
      estimated profit and loss statement, in reasonable detail, for the current operating
      year; and (b) estimates for such operating year, covering expenditures for: (i)
      repairs and maintenance; (ii) the purchase of operating equipment; (iii) the purchase
      of furnishings and equipment; (iv) capital improvements; and (v) advertising and
      business promotion costs of the Facility;

(d)   Provide for the retention of and the appropriate monitoring of independent
      accountants and attorneys to timely prepare and file any and all required state and
      federal tax returns, appeals, etc.;

(e)   Perform on behalf of the Facility and jointly with the other facilities with whom
      Cedarbridge Service Consultant contracts from time to time, group purchasing and
      procurement of medical equipment, appliances, supplies, food and other items, as
      directed by the Licensee;

(f)   Provide or arrange with third parties, at Licensee’s cost, to provide all payroll
      functions including all regulatory and tax reporting functions relating to payroll
      administration.

(g)   Assist Licensee in establishing and maintaining banking relationships, including
      securing loans on behalf of Licensee, complying with the loan terms, supplying
      banks with any and all necessary documentation to properly document and support
      loan levels and otherwise manage Licensee’s cash flow and cash management
      needs, to the extent of Licensee’s cash availability. As part of its cash management
      services, Cedarbridge Service Consultant may, with the consent of Licensee and
      other facilities, arrange for and process loans between Licensee and its affiliates in
      a manner that minimizes the interest and financing costs to Licensee and allows for
      the orderly cash management of funds for Licensee. Cedarbridge Service
      Consultant shall properly document any such loans and shall implement same only
      with the written consent of Licensee and such other facilities. Nothing contained
      herein shall require Cedarbridge Service Consultant to advance any sums to or for
      the benefit of Licensee;

(h)   Establish bank accounts in Licensee’s name for the Facility at banking or other
      financial institution(s) reasonably acceptable to Licensee. The Cedarbridge Service
      Consultant shall deposit in such accounts all monies received in the name of
      Licensee from the operation of the Facility and will disburse the same for the
      purposes set forth in (k) below with signature authority over bank accounts to which

===== PAGE 50 =====
            governmental accounts receivable shall be deposited being vested solely in
            Licensee;

     (i)    From the accounts referred to in (j) above or from other monies of Licensee, pay
            on behalf of, and in the name of Licensee, such amounts and at such times as are
            required in connection with the leasing, operation, management or maintenance of
            the Facility;

     (j)    Cedarbridge Service Consultant is empowered by the Licensee and authorized to
            act as its agent to negotiate rates of reimbursement from any and all applicable
            governmental agencies and non-governmental entities, and shall provide all books,
            records, and other financial information or any related audits thereof;

     (k)    Cedarbridge Service Consultant may, assist the Licensee – or its billing agency to,
            institute any and all actions or proceedings to collect charges or other income due
            to the Facility and to identify and make refunds or submit corrected claims to third
            party payers and residents as required by applicable law or contract, disclosures
            relating to claims, responses to additional development requests, payer audits,
            recoupments including pursuing appeals therefrom, as provided however, Licensee
            is responsible for the submission of all claims for reimbursement including without
            limitation compliance with all conditions of participation, conditions of payment,
            medical necessity requirements, documentation requirements, coverage
            requirements, or other billing requirements.

     (l)    Assist Licensee in preparing, maintaining and updating Licensees licensing and
            provider enrollment and relating filings.

     (m)    Assist Licensee on the ongoing maintenance, development and improvement of the
            Information Technology system to appropriately oversee confidential Facility
            functions including banking, treasury, cash management, risk management,
            workers’ compensation, therapy services, payroll, insurance, data,
            patient/employee data, NJS transmissions, attorney-client work products, etc.;

     (n)    Retain outside legal counsel to perform all legal services for Licensee; and

     (o)    Cedarbridge Service Consultant will make itself available to assist, advise and
            perform such other duties as may reasonably be requested by Licensee.

3.   Delegation. Service Consultant may delegate to other persons and/or entities of its
     choosing, such aspects and portions of its responsibility and duty, and power and authority
     it deems appropriate.

4.   Service Consultant’s Covenants. Service Consultant shall at all times comply with any
     and all rules and laws governing the Facility including but not limited to the prohibition on
     paying or receiving any remuneration relating to the provision of services to any person for
     whom reimbursement will be paid by any governmental or non-governmental third party
     payer. Additionally, Service Consultant shall at all times be familiar with and comply with
     Licensee’s compliance plan. Service Consultant represents and warrants that it and its

     respective personnel: (i) are, and will remain at all times throughout the term of this
     Agreement, authorized to participate in the Medicare and state Medicaid programs and
     shall comply with all conditions of participation or other requirements applicable to
     participation in such programs; (ii)have, and will maintain at all times throughout the term
     of this Agreement, all the necessary qualifications, certifications, licenses and/or
     accreditations required by federal, state and local laws and regulations to provide the
     services covered by this Agreement; (iii) will provide the Services in accordance with the
     professional standards and principles applicable to their profession; (iv) are not, and at no
     time have been, excluded from participation in any federally funded health care program,
     including Medicare or Medicaid, or sanctioned under any appliance state or federal fraud
     and abuse statutes; and (v) are not subject to any revocation, suspension or other restriction
     on the privilege, authority or right to bill any third-party payer including without limitation
     the Medicare or Medicaid Programs. Background checks shall be conducted on initial hire
     and on an ongoing basis as required by applicable law. If any party or personnel providing
     services under this agreement no longer holds a valid, required license or is excluded under
     this section, the other party shall be immediately notified..

5.   Independent Contractor.

     (a)    Notwithstanding anything to the contrary contained in this Agreement, the parties
            hereto mutually acknowledge and agree that Cedarbridge Service Consultant is an
            independent contractor and not an employee of Licensee.

1.   Licensee’s Duties. Licensee shall perform the following:

     (a)    Licensee agrees that it will cooperate with Cedarbridge Service Consultant in
            connection with Cedarbridge Service Consultant’s performance of its duties and
            obligations, and/or its exercise of its rights, privileges and/or authority hereunder;
            and

     (b)    Timely pay Cedarbridge Service Consultant its compensation in accordance with
            Section 7 hereof, and indemnify it in accordance with Section 9 hereof; and,

     (c)    Frequently, regularly and timely advise Service Consultant regarding the
            Licensee’s operations at the Facility as necessary to ensure that the Facility is
            operating in accordance with this requirement and applicable law and third-party
            payer requirements.

2.   Compensation and Expenses.

     (a)    The Licensee agrees to pay to Cedarbridge Service Consultant as compensation for
            services rendered by it under this Agreement an amount of $30,000.00 per month
            on the first day of the month following execution.

     (b)    Additionally, Licensee shall reimburse Cedarbridge Service Consultant for all
            out-of-pocket expenditures incurred by Cedarbridge Service Consultant in
            connection with performing its obligations and the Services under this Agreement.

3.   Licensee’s Licenses and Operations.

     (a)   The parties agree that Licensee is, and at all times during the Term hereof shall
           remain, the licensed operator of the Facility, and that Cedarbridge Service
           Consultant is only a Cedarbridge Service Consultant and is not responsible for the
           operations of the Facility, including, without limitation: (a) the management and
           supervision of the Facility’s employees and staff, (b) the delivery/provision of
           patient care, (c) the adoption or implementation of the Facility’s policies and
           procedures, (d) the setting or implementation of staffing patterns, (e) the setting or
           implementation of any functions relating to the operation of the Facility, including
           but not limited to nursing, dietary, activities and housekeeping, and/or (f) the
           maintenance of the physical plant, equipment, capital assets and/or grounds located
           at the Facility; all of which shall remain the sole and exclusive responsibility and
           liability of Licensee at all times.

     (b)   The parties agree that Licensee shall obtain all licenses and permits necessary for
           operation of the Facility. Such licenses shall be in the name of the Licensee, and
           maintained at Licensee’s expense. Licensee shall be fully responsible for applying
           for, obtaining and maintaining such licenses and permits. Licensee shall retain
           outside legal counsel, at Licensee’s expense, to represent the Facility in connection
           with all regulatory matters including but not limited to responding and defending
           against findings, deficiencies and violations issued by applicable state, federal and
           regulatory bodies and Cedarbridge Service Consultant shall have no responsibility
           with respect thereto.

     (c)   Licensee represents and warrants that it and its respective personnel: (i) are, and
           will remain at all times throughout the term of this Agreement, authorized to
           participate in the Medicare and state Medicaid programs and shall comply with all
           conditions of participation or other requirements applicable to participation in such
           programs.; (ii) have, and will maintain at all times throughout the term of this
           Agreement, all necessary qualifications, certifications, licenses and/or
           accreditations required by federal, state and local laws and regulations to provide
           the services covered by this Agreement; (iii) will provide the Services in
           accordance with the professional standards and principles applicable to their
           profession; (iv) are not, and at no time have been, excluded from participation in
           any federally funded health care program, including Medicare or Medicaid, or
           sanctioned under any applicable state or federal fraud and abuse statutes; and (v)
           are not subject to any revocation, suspension or other restriction on the privilege,
           authority or right to bill any third party payer including without limitation the
           Medicare or Medicaid Programs. Background checks shall be conducted on initial
           hire and on an ongoing basis as required by applicable law. If any party or personnel
           providing services under this agreement no longer holds a valid, required license or
           is excluded under this section, the other party shall be immediately notified.

     (d)   The parties agree that Licensee shall at all times continue to exercise control over
           the assets and operations of the Facility, and Cedarbridge Service Consultant shall
           perform its responsibilities as described in this Agreement in accordance with the

      consent of the owning party.

                      ADMINISTRATIVE SERVICES AGREEMENT

        This Administrative Services Agreement (“Agreement”), is effective as of this 8th day of
March, 2021, by and between Lincolnwood Operator LLC (“Licensed Operator”), and Marquis
Health Consulting Services LLC (the “Administrative Services Company”), (collectively the
“Parties”).

       The Parties acknowledge receipt of valid consideration including the mutual promises
contained herein. Intending to be legally bound the Parties agree as follows:

I. Recitals

       A. The Licensed Operator is passionate about caring for residents and desires to provide
          high quality resident care and focus on clinical issues.
       B. The Licensed Nursing Home Administrator (the “Licensed Administrator”) manages
          all functions of the Licensed Operator and in many cases, such as for Nursing,
          Restorative Therapy, and Social Services Departments has licensed Clinicians who
          lead those departments under his or her guidance and direction.
       C. The Licensed Operator has other Department heads who are likewise managed by the
          Licensed Administrator for activities, physical plant, and the like, who have experience
          and expertise in their specific areas of operations.
       D. The Licensed Operator, the Licensed Administrator and the Department Heads have a
          multitude of resources available to them including the people who work at the Facility
          and in their departments as well as dozens of vendors and consultants.
       E. The Licensed Operator has a business office which works with financial service
          companies and accountants to bill, collect receivables, pay payable, keep books and
          records and to perform other back-office business related functions.
       F. The Licensed Operator desires for a consultant to assist it with organizational structure
          and more effectively utilize some of the foregoing resources as set forth herein.
       G. The Licensed Operator shall control and remain responsible for all matters relating to
          its day-to-day operations and the operation of the Facility.

       WHEREAS the Parties agree as follows:

II.    Relationship between the Parties

       A. The Licensed Operator has appointed and shall employ a licensed administrator
          (“Licensed Administrator”) who is responsible for management of the Facility.
       B. The Administrator is and shall remain, without regard to any input or contribution from
          the Administrative Services Company, solely responsible for ensuring: (i) the
          existence, adequacy, and proper implementation of all required and/or prudent policies
          and procedures; (ii) compliance with all rules, regulations, laws, governmental



                                                1

===== PAGE 64 =====
   directives; (iii) compliance with all terms of its license(s) provider agreements and
   contracts; (iv) that it meets all applicable standards of care.



C. The Administrative Services Company is not in any way responsible for clinical, legal,
   accounting, tax or other services or advice that require specialized training, licensing
   and/or education. The foregoing is true even if the employees of the Administrative
   Services Company hold advanced and/or specialized degrees and/or certifications as
   the Administrative Service Company shall not provide professional advice and/or usurp
   the power, authority, or responsibility of those within or outside the facility who do
   offer professional advice or services.

D. The Licensed Operator shall maintain its independence as an operator, employer, and
   caregivers and does not hereby cede its independence or responsibilities to the
   Administrative Services Company in any way. Without limiting the generality of the
   foregoing, Licensed Operator shall make independent decisions concerning how to
   meet or exceed each and every duty of care and responsibility relating to: (i) residents,
   staff and visitors to the facility; (ii) governmental entities and authorities; (iii) any and
   all loans policy of insurance, and/or any other contract; (iv) employer/employee
   relationship and benefits; (v) workplace and/or safety; (vi) resident trust accounts, (vii)
   Payrol and/or withholding, (viii) any other responsibility it has as an owner, operator,
   tenant, lessee, borrower, employer, holder of any license and/or provider agreement
   and/or healthcare provider.
E. The Licensed Operator shall make all final decisions relating to: (i) patient/resident
   care, (ii) staffing, (iii) resources, (iv) billing, (v) admissions, (vi) discharge, (vii)
   budgets and in all ways remain responsible for the adoption and implementation of
   those decisions.
F. Without regard to its actions or inactions, the Administrative Services Company is
   hereby assuming no liability for any matters consisting of or relating to the areas set
   forth in Paragraphs II(A-E).

G. The Administrative Services Company may share non-protected external information
   and/or data with the Licensed Operator that it obtains from other customers and/or
   industry sources. The Licensed Operator hereby consents to permitting the
   Administrative Services Company to share non-protected information and/or data with
   other of its clients and/or industry sources to the extent it obtains the same from the
   Facility. The purpose of sharing information and data with others is to permit the
   Licensed Operators to have access to real time information that is greater than itself.
H. The Licensed Operator hereby agrees that it shall not misuse this information and/or
   data. Licensed Operator acknowledges that every facility has different: (i) physical


                                           2

===== PAGE 65 =====
   characteristics, (ii) residents, staff and community demographics, (iii) residents’
   diagnosis and acuity, (iv) leadership, (v) case mix , (vi) payor mix, (viii) marketplace,
   (ix) vendors, consultants and other external support, and (vii) is different in a multitude
   of other ways. Under no circumstances shall any comparative data be interpreted as an
   “expectation” to conform to the norm or to otherwise have the Facility attempt to
   change its processes and/or outcomes as a result of receipt of any comparative data.
   Comparative data may be used to spur discussions and thought.
I. The Administrative Services Company may likewise provide the Licensed Operator
   with ways to view its own real time and comparatively data and/or assist the Licensed
   Operator to obtain a vendor who has the ability to do the same. The use of the foregoing
   may vary but again, care must be driven by the needs of each individual resident and
   not reports. Reports however may be used to spur discussion concerning why outcome
   is different than predictive models or why trends or statistical anomalies exist.
   Sometimes reporting may compare actual to budgeted numbers. Budgets are merely
   expectations based upon predictive analysis. When the predictive analysis and reality
   materially deviate it is an opportunity for discussion of process, and/or any need to
   modify the predictive analysis for the future. In no way shall resident care be changed
   solely because it does or does not conform to any budget or other predictive analysis.
J. The Facility warrants that care will, without regard to advice and/or suggestions from
   the Administrative Services Company, be driven by patient’s needs and governmental
   requirements. While the Administrative Services Company may be involved in
   preparing or distributing predictive analysis, it is not responsible for the clinical
   programs or care at the Facility or directing the Clinical staff.
K. The Licensed Operator shall be solely responsible for ensuring that each and every
   person and/or vendor of the facility that comes into contact with comparative data
   understands the foregoing and does not misconstrue the supply of comparative data or
   non-clinical advice as pressure to conform and does not change its conduct solely as a
   result of where the Facility’s numbers sit with regard to others or against its own
   predictive or historical data.. Subject to the foregoing, the data may be used or
   disregarded by the Licensed Operator in any way it desires and will be without liability
   to the Administrative Services Company.
L. Every communication from the Administrative Services Company or any of its officers,
   directors, employees, agents, servants, independent contractors or the like shall be
   deemed to have the following qualifiers “WITHOUT REGARD TO THIS
   COMMUNICATION: DO NOT DO ANYTHING IN CONTRAVENTION OF THE
   LAW; DO NOT ACT IN VIOLATION OF YOUR LEGAL OR CIVIL
   OBLIGATIONS; AND DO NOT ACT UNETHICALLY.”
M. By entering this Agreement, the Parties intend for the Licensed Operator, and not the
   Administrative Services Company, to have responsibility for ensuring legality and/or
   propriety of actions taken or inactions of the Licensed Operator.

   work with other vendors. The Administrative Services Company shall not, as a result

O. The Licensed Operator is solely responsible for the implementation and approval of
   any and all budgets. Any role the Administrative Services Company is ministerial
   and/or advisory and is done with the express acknowledgement that the same is without
   liability to the Administrative Service Company.

P. The Administrative Services Company is not and shall not be deemed a manager,

VIII.        Term.

      A. Unless sooner terminated pursuant hereto, the term of this Agreement begins on the
         date hereof and shall continue for one (1) year (the “Term”). The Agreement shall
         automatically be renewed from term to term.

      B. Termination for Cause: The Licensed Operator and/or the Administrative Services
         Company may terminate the Agreement for cause with thirty (30) days written notice,
         should: (i) the Licensed Operator of the facility cease to be the current licensed
         operator; or (ii) should the other party materially breach this agreement. In such case
         of termination for cause, the Licensed Operator shall be obligated for payment to the
         Administrative Services Company through the date of termination and neither party
         shall have any obligation to the other, except for any obligations to defend indemnify
         and/or hold the other harmless relating to the period after the date of termination.

      C. Termination without Cause. The Licensed Operator and/or the Administrative Services
         Company may terminate this Agreement without cause upon sixty (60) days prior
         written notice to Licensed Operator or to the Administrative Services Company as the
         case may be. In such case of termination without cause, the Licensed Operator shall
         be obligated for payment to the Administrative Services Company through the date of
         termination and neither party shall have any obligation to the other, except for any
         obligations to defend indemnify and/or hold the other harmless relating to the period
         after the date of termination.


IX.       Fee.

      A. As compensation for the services set forth herein, the Licensed Operator shall pay to
         the Administrative Services Company the fees as set forth on Schedule A (the “Fees”).
         The Fees shall be paid monthly.

      B. Notwithstanding the Fee schedule set forth on Schedule A, in the event that the Fees
         charged are less than the cost of services rendered, the Parties shall equitably increase
         the Fees to be no less than the Administrative Services Company’s cost of providing
         services to the Licensed Operator.

      C. In the event that the Licensed Operator fails to pay the foregoing Fee as and when due,
         the Administrative Services Company shall be entitled to all costs of collections from
         the Licensed Operator including without limiting the generality of the foregoing, all
         sheriff fees, collection company fees, attorney, paralegal, accounting and all other costs

         and expenses of any kind incurred. Past due balances shall also be subject to interest
         at a rate of the lesser of 1.5% per month or the highest rate permitted by law.

                                          EXHIBIT A

                                       FEE SCHEDULE



               (a)    Marquis Health Consulting Services will bill the Operator on or about the
  th
30 of each month for services rendered during said month. The Operator shall make payments
to Marquis Health Consulting Services on or before the 15th of the month immediately following
the month in which the services were rendered. Marquis Health Consulting Services’
compensation shall be in the amount equal to six percent (6%) of the Operator’s Gross Revenues
(the “Fee”) for each month minus fees paid to vendors relating to (a) billing and collections
services, (b) “back office” accounting services and (c) clinical support and general oversight
services. Gross Revenue determined for any period shall be in accordance with generally accepted
accounting principles consistently applied, including but not limited to all collected revenues and
income of any kind derived directly from Facility operations (including collected rentals or other
payments from concessionaires, licenses, tenants, and other users of the Facility, but not gross
receipts of such concessionaires, licenses, tenants or uses), excluding:

               i.       Federal, state, and municipal excise, sales and use taxes, collected directly
                        from patients as a part of the sales price of any goods or services;
               ii.      Proceeds of any life insurance policies;
             iii.       Gains arising from the sale or other disposition of capital assets;
              iv.       Any reversal of any contingency or tax reserve;
               v.       Advances on the line of credit or other borrowed funds;
              vi.       Interest earnings from agency or other accounts; and
             vii.       Gifts of any kind or nature to the Operator.


To illustrate, Marquis Health Consulting Services will bill for its services for the month of
February 2021, on or about January 30, 2021, and payment shall be due by February 15, 2021.

               (b)    The parties hereto agree that the compensation provided in this Section is
consistent with fair market value in an arm’s-length transaction and is not and has not been
determined in a manner that takes into account the volume or value of any referrals or business
otherwise generated for or with respect to Operator or between the parties or any of the respective
owners thereof for which payment may be made in whole or in part under Medicare or any state
health care program or under any other payor program.

         THIS AGREEMENT FOR ASSIGNMENT OF MEMBERSHIP INTEREST (this

Quinto Delta LLC a New Jersey limited liability company ("Assignor") and Quinto Nexgen LLC
a New Jersey limited liability company (“Quinto”) and UKR Nexgen LLC a New Jersey limited
liability company (“UKR”) (collectively the "Assignee").

                                             RECITALS

                               WITNESSETH, THAT WHEREAS:

         A.       Assignor owns 88.6% membership interest (the “Member Interest”) in
Lincolnwood Operator LLC, a Rhode Island limited liability company (the "Company"), governed
by that certain agreement captioned dated as of January 1, 2021 (the “Operating Agreement”) by
and between Assignor, the Company and other parties.

          B.       Assignor desires to assign 78.6% of the membership interest in the Company to
Quinto and the remaining 10% to UKR and each Assignee desires to accept such assignment and
to assume all of the duties and obligations as a member of the Company on the terms and subject
to the provisions set forth in the Amended and Restated Operating Agreement and this Assignment.

          NOW, THEREFORE, for good and valuable consideration and in consideration of the promises
and covenants set forth herein, the receipt and sufficiency of which are hereby acknowledged, the parties
agree as follows:

       1. Assignor agrees to sell to Assignee its Member Interest in the Company, and Assignee
          does hereby agree to purchase the Member Interest from Assignor upon the terms and
          conditions set forth in this Assignment.

       2. The Closing on this assignment shall occur on December 31, 2021.

       3. The purchase price for the assignment of the Member Interest shall be the market value
          of the Member Interest as determined by an appraiser prior to the Closing.

                        CHARITY CARE POLICY & PROCEDURE

                                   Lincolnwood Operator, LLC


Purpose:
It shall be the policy of Lincolnwood Operator LLC (“Provider”) to provide free care to Residents on
a case-by-case basis, based on facility financial ability.
Qualifying Residents:
Provider will provide free care to those Residents who have a medical need for Provider's services
and who meet the charity care guidelines as outlined herein ("Qualifying Residents"). Accordingly,
Qualifying Residents will be those uninsured or underinsured Residents with incomes up to 125% of
the Federal Poverty Limits and who are without readily available funds that could be used to pay for
their care. (Qualifying Residents must have applied for any governmental assistance programs for
which they qualify. If a Qualifying Resident is not eligible for governmental assistance or is denied
governmental assistance, such Resident may apply for charity care.)
Charity Care Policy:
Provider agrees it will neither bill Qualifying Residents for services directly supplied by Provider nor
collect any fees for provision of such services. Services that are provided under contract with an
outside provider shall not be eligible for charity care and will be billed in the customary manner,
unless otherwise explicitly agreed to in writing by the outside provider.
Monitoring:
A summary of charity care provided will be drafted and provided to management on an annual basis.

WW
West Gables

REHABILITATION & HEALTHCARE CENTER
July 29, 2021

To Whom It May Concern,

This letter serves to provide you with information pursuant to the immediate jeopardy
citation received by West Gables Healthcare Center (Facility) during the Standard Certification
Survey commencing on April 9, 2021. The Facility received F880 at a “K” Level deficiency for
failure to appropriately disinfect the blood glucose monitoring device/glucometer that was
being used for multiple residents. The failure to appropriately disinfect the blood glucose
monitoring device/glucometer increased the potential likelihood of cross contamination of

bloodborne pathogens for residents

The Facility received notification of the immediate jeopardy on April 7, 2021. An

immediate removal plan was implemented which included:

e The nurses identified in the deficient practice
o Received re-education by the Director of Nursing on the appropriate cleaning
and disinfection of blood glucose monitoring devices/glucometers.
o Completed a competency with return demonstration to ensure appropriate
cleaning and disinfection of blood glucose monitoring devices/glucometers.
e All Licensed Nurses
o Received education on the policy and procedure for the cleaning and disinfection

of the disinfection of blood glucose monitoring devices/glucometers.

P: 305.262.6800 | F:305.265.5021 | admissions@westgablesrehab.com
2525 SW 75th Avenue, Miami, FL 33155 | WestGablesRehab.com

===== PAGE 92 =====
[OCR]
Y

West. Gables

REHABILITATION & HEALTHCARE CENTER

© Completed a competency with return demonstration to validate appropriate use,
including cleaning and disinfection, of the blood glucose monitoring
devices/glucometers.
e Education and competencies blood glucose monitoring devices/glucometers have been
added to the orientation process and annual education for licensed nurses.
e All medication carts were audited to ensure appropriate cleaning and disinfection
supplies were available.
e All medication carts were audited to ensure two blood glucose monitoring
devices/glucometers were available.
e The Director of Nursing conducted audits to ensure compliance with appropriate use,
including cleaning and disinfection, of blood glucose monitoring devices/glucometers.
e The results of the audits were reviewed through the Quality Assurance Performance

Improvement committee to ensure compliance.

The immediate jeopardy was removed on April 8, 2021; the F880 was reduced to a level
“E”. The Facility provided a plan of correction for the deficient practice alleging compliance on
May 9, 2021. The plan of correction was accepted, and a revisit was conducted on May 14,
2021 verifying compliance.

The Facility takes the results of the survey very seriously. The immediate jeopardy
received by West Gables Healthcare Center was an isolated incident and not a pattern of

deficient practice. It is not expected to be repeated and not reflective of the expected survey

outcomes.
