                                                   ARTICLE 4
                                             CAPITAL CONTRIBUTIONS
          1. Initial Capital Contributions. All Capital Contributions of the Company shall be contributed in accordance
with the Membership Interests of the Members. The Manager does not, in any way, guarantee the return of the Members’
Capital Contributions or a profit for the Members from the operations of the Company. In addition to the Capital
Contributions of the Members, upon acquisition of the Company’s property, the Company may borrow additional funds
from all of the Members or an affiliate of the Company which loan shall be evidenced by a note and shall be repaid as set
forth therein.

          2. Estimated Expenses. The Manager shall, from time to time, but not more often than quarterly, estimate the
cash requirements of the Company for an ensuing period of time and the Members shall each be responsible to pay their
pro rata share of such estimated cash requirements in accordance with the Members’ Membership Interests.
          3. Members Failure to Make Contributions. Upon demand by the Manager, each Member agrees to pay,
within ten (10) days after receiving a demand therefore, its pro rata share (based upon such Member’s interest in the
Company pursuant to Section 3(1) and irrespective of the percentage of the Initial Capital Contributions of each Member)
of all costs, expenses or charges with respect to the ownership, construction, operation, maintenance and upkeep of the
Company’s property to the extent that all of said costs, expenses or charges cannot be paid out of the cash flow (as herein
defined) if any, of the Company. The provisions of this paragraph shall be binding upon the Members and the Company,
but such provisions shall not inure to the benefit of any other person including any creditors of the Company. Any
distribution due to a Member who has defaulted in payment of any required Capital Contribution shall be first applied to
any reimbursement owed by that defaulting Member to the Company plus interest at the rate of eighteen (18%) percent or
the highest amount allowed by law whichever is less, which shall compound monthly, pursuant to the heter iska in Section
9(12) below (the “Heter Iska”), or to another Member who has made the Capital Contributions on the part of the defaulting
Member plus interest as provided for in Section 5 of this Article, before distribution of the remainder, if any, to the
defaulting Member.
          4. Option to Assist Defaulting Member. If any Member shall default in the payment of any sums required
to be paid by it to the Company hereunder (the “Defaulting Member”), the Manager shall notify the other Members
regarding such default and the other Members may (the “Assisting Member”), without the Company waiving any claims
for breach, cure such default for the account of the Defaulting Member (the “Default Loan”). The Defaulting Member
shall be responsible to repay (solely out of any distribution to which the defaulting Member may be entitled) the Default
Loan with interest at the lesser of eighteen (18%) percent or the highest amount allowed by law which shall compound
monthly and shall be subject to the Heter Iska. Until the Default Loan is repaid in full, with interest as herein provided, all
distributions which would otherwise have been made to the Defaulting Member shall be paid to the Assisting Member, to
be applied first to accrued interest and then to principal. Any distribution to the Assisting Member in connection with the
Default Loan pursuant to this Paragraph (or to the Company pursuant to Section 4 of this Article) shall be considered a
distribution to the Defaulting Member. In the event more than one Member wishes to make the Default Loan, such
Members shall each make a loan to the Defaulting Member pro rata based on the percentage interest each such Assisting
Member has to the combined Membership Interests of all Assisting Members. All amounts contributed by the Assisting
Members pursuant to this Section shall be paid within ten (10) days of the delivery by the Manager of the notice of default.
          5. Priority of Capital Contributions. Except as may be expressly provided in this Agreement, no Member
shall have priority over any other Member, either for the return of Capital Contributions or for net profits, net losses, or
distributions; provided that this Section shall not apply to loans (as distinguished from Capital Contributions) which a
Member has made to the Company.
          6. Loans. The Manager shall have the right to borrow funds on behalf of the Company at the best available
terms, including from any Member, at any time that the Company needs funds for the continued operations thereof for a
period of ninety (90) days or less. Any loan from a Member shall accrue interest from the Company at the then Applicable




                                                              4

===== PAGE 163 =====
Federal Rate announced by the Internal Revenue Service plus eight (8%) percent per annum (but in no event more than
18%) pursuant to the Heter Iska and any payments on such loan shall be first used to pay interest and then to principal.


                                                    ARTICLE 5
                                                   MANAGEMENT
          1. Management. The management of the Company shall be by a Manager, who shall have the sole authority
to bind the Company. Except as set forth in Subsection 3, the Manager shall have the authority to make all decisions for
the Company and all such decisions of the Manager shall be binding upon the Company.
         2. Certain Powers of Manager. Subject to the provisions of Subsection 3, the Manager shall have the
exclusive power and authority, on behalf of the Company, in accordance with Section 1 above:
                  (a) Subject to the restrictions set forth in Subsection 3, to borrow for the Company from banks, other
         lending institutions, Persons, Members, or affiliates of the Manager or Members on such terms as the Manager
         deems appropriate so long as such terms are commercially reasonable, and in connection therewith, to
         hypothecate, encumber, and grant security interests in the assets of the Company to secure repayment of the
         borrowed sums. No debt shall be contracted or liability incurred by or on behalf of the Company except by the
         Manager or, to the extent permitted under the Act, by agents or employees of the Company expressly authorized
         to contract such debt or to incur such liability by the Manager;
                  (b) To purchase liability and other insurance to protect the Company’s property and business;
                  (c) To hold and own any Company real and/or personal properties in the name of the Company;
                  (d) To time open bank accounts in the name of the Company, and the Manager shall be the sole
         signatory thereon, unless the Manager determines otherwise.
                  (e) To invest any Company funds temporarily (by way of example but not limitation) in time deposits,
         short-term governmental obligations, commercial paper, or other investments;
                   (f) To execute on behalf of the Company all instruments and documents, including, without limitation:
         checks; drafts; notes and other negotiable instruments; mortgages, or deeds of trust; security agreements;
         financing statements; documents providing for the acquisition, mortgage or disposition of the Company’s
         property; assignments; bills of sale; leases; Company agreements; operating agreements of other entities; and any
         other instruments or documents necessary, in the opinion of the Manager, to the business of the Company;
                   (g) To employ accountants, legal counsel, managing agents, brokers, or other experts to perform
         services for the Company and to compensate them from Company funds. The fact that a Manager or a Member




                                                            5

===== PAGE 164 =====
         is directly or indirectly affiliated or connected with any such employed party shall not prohibit the Manager from
         employing that party so long as the terms of such employment are commercially reasonable;
                  (h) To enter into any and all other agreements on behalf of the Company, with any person for any
         purpose, in such forms as the Manager may approve;
                   (i) To act as an attorney in fact under any power of attorney given to the Company by any person or
         entity; and
                 (j) To perform any capital improvements, rehabilitation or other work at the Property as determined
         by the Manager and to execute any contracts in connection with same.
                  (k) To determine whether to make any capital calls or distributions (which shall be in accordance with
         this Agreement, and the amount thereof and to maintain reserves for the Company.
                (l) To do and perform all other acts as may be necessary or appropriate to the conduct of the
         Company’s business.


         3. Limitation of Manager Powers. Notwithstanding anything to the contrary contained in this Agreement, the
Manager shall have no authority to do any of the following without the written consent of the Members holding a majority
of the Membership Interests in the Company (the “Member Actions”):
                  (a) Sell all or substantially all of the Company’s assets in a single transaction or a series of related
                      transactions;

                  (b) Make any capital calls or distributions;

                  (c) Approve the transfer of Membership Interests;

                  (d) Amend this Agreement;

                  (e) Borrow any funds from a Member, affiliate of a Member or any party related to any Member;

                  (f) Borrow from a third party, aside from in the normal course of business such as trade payables,
                      equipment financing and the like;

                  (g) Admit additional Members to the Company;

                  (h) Hire any immediate family member of any direct or indirect owner of the Manager (but excluding
                      any hire for which the compensation is equal to the market and for which the salary thereof is less
                      than $150,000) or enter into any agreement in which the indirect owners of the Manager have any
                      interest, unless such interest is pro rata to the other Members of the Company;

                  (i) Enter into any employment contract or otherwise hire an employee who is not an at will employee;

                  (j) Enter into any contract whose term is more than one (1) year unless terminable with thirty (30)
                      days notice, or enter into any contract which is not in the normal course of business.

         4. Reserved Powers. Unless authorized to do so by this Agreement or by the Manager of the Company,
no attorney-in-fact, employee, or other agent of the Company shall have any power or authority to bind the Company
in any way, to pledge its credit or to render it liable peculiarly for any purpose. No Member shall have any power or
authority to bind the Company unless the Member is the Manager or has been authorized by the Manager to act as an
agent of the Company in accordance herewith.
          5. Liability for Certain Acts. The Manager shall perform its duties in good faith, in a manner it reasonably
believes to be in the best interest of the Company, and with such care as an ordinarily prudent person in a like position
would use under similar circumstances. The Manager who so performs the duties of Manager shall not have any liability
by reason of being or having been a Manager of the Company. The Manager does not, in any way, guarantee the return



                                                            6

===== PAGE 165 =====
of the Members’ Capital Contributions or a profit for the member from the operations of the Company. The Manager shall
not be liable to the Company or to any Member for any loss or damage sustained by the Company or any Member, unless
the loss or damage shall have been the result fraud, willful misconduct or a wrongful taking by the Manager (each of the
above a “Bad Act”).
           6. Indemnity. To the fullest extent permitted by the Act, the Company shall indemnify, defend and hold
harmless each person and entity (an "Indemnified Person") who was or is made a party or is threatened to be made a
party to or is involved in any threatened, pending or completed action, suit or proceeding, whether civil, criminal,
administrative, arbitrative or investigative (including, without limitation, an action by or in the right of the Company), or
any appeal in such a proceeding or any inquiry or investigation that could lead to such a proceeding, by reason of the fact
that he, or a person or entity of whom he is the personal representative, is or was an officer, Manager, Member,
employee or agent of the Company, or is or was serving as a director, member, manager, officer, partner, venturer,
proprietor, trustee, employee or agent of another limited liability company, corporation, partnership, joint venture, trust,
sole proprietorship, employee benefit plan or other enterprise, that is or was a Manager or Member or which has any
direct or indirect interest in the Company or any manager and all affiliates of such Indemnified Person and any entity,
whether or not affiliated with such Indemnified Person, that a judgment against such entity will have an adverse effect on
any Indemnified Person, from and against any liabilities, expenses (including, without limitation, attorneys' fees and
expenses and any other costs and expenses incurred in connection with defending such action, suit or proceeding),
judgments, penalties, fines and amounts paid in settlement actually and reasonably incurred by such Indemnified Person
in connection with such proceeding except that the provisions of this Section shall not be applicable to any liability
which is the result of a Bad Act. The Company shall indemnify its Manager and its agents from any claims alleging a
breach of duty to the Company or its Members unless it is determined that an act or omission was intentionally (a) in
breach of such Person’s duty of loyalty to the Company or its Members (b) not in good faith or involving a knowing
violation of law or (c) resulted in the receipt of an improper benefit by such Person. Company shall name the foregoing
entities and individuals for whom it is required to indemnify as additional insureds on its general and professional
liability insurance.

           7. Manager Has No Exclusive Duty to Company. The Manager shall not be required to manage the
Company as its sole and exclusive function and it may have other business interests and may engage in other activities in
addition to those relating to the Company including any activities which directly compete with the Company. Neither the
Company nor any Member shall have any right, by virtue of this Agreement, to share or participate in such other
investments or activities of the Manager or to the income or proceeds derived therefrom. The Manager shall incur no
liability to the Company or to any of the Members as a result of engaging in any other business or venture. The Manager
shall have the right, on commercially reasonable terms, to hire an affiliate to perform all capital repairs, maintenance,
construction, rehabilitation and any other work at the Property or to perform such repairs, maintenance, construction,
rehabilitation or other work on its own, but shall have no obligation to do so.
          8. Additional Members. At any time, Quinto shall have the right to convey a portion of its Membership
Interests to any party upon any terms so long as Quinto and its affiliates collectively continue to own a majority of all the
Membership Interests which it owned on the date hereof.
         9. Reimbursements. The Company will reimburse the Manager or its affiliate for all actual and reasonable
costs and expenses incurred in the course of its service to the Company hereunder including any salary and other
compensation paid to its employees who work at the property and who perform legal, financial analysis and bookkeeping
work.
          10. Resignation. The Manager may resign at any time by giving written notice to the Members of the Company.
The resignation of the Manager shall take effect upon receipt of the notice or at such later time as shall be specified in the
notice; and, unless otherwise specified in the notice, the acceptance of the resignation shall not be necessary to make it
effective. The resignation of a Manager who is also a Member shall not affect the Manager’s rights as a Member and shall
not constitute a withdrawal of a Member.
         11. Removal and Replacement. The Manager may be removed at any time, for any reason or no reason, by
the Majority Member or if no Member holds a majority interest then by the Members who jointly hold a majority of the
Membership Interests. The Majority Member shall have the right to fill any vacancy in the Manager position (such
manager the “Replacement Manager” and any reference in this Agreement to the Manager shall be the Replacement
Manager). The powers of the Replacement Manager shall be not be limited by the terms of Section 3 of this Article and
the Replacement Manager shall have the power to make all decisions on behalf of the Company and the Company shall



                                                              7

===== PAGE 166 =====
be bound by the decisions of the Replacement Manager, unless the Member holding the majority of the Membership
Interests shall otherwise elect and shall so state in writing evidencing the appointment of the Replacement Manager.] Any
officer may be removed by the Manager or by the Majority Member at any time and in such event the Manager or majority
of the members shall appoint a new officer.
                                                            ARTICLE 6
                                       ALLOCATIONS AND DISTRIBUTIONS
         1. Allocation of Profits. Allocations of profit shall be made as follows: (a) items of net income and gain
shall be allocated among the Members in the manner necessary to increase each Member’s Capital Account to an
amount equal to the amount of cash such Member would be entitled to receive pursuant to subsections (6) of this
Article if an amount of cash equal to the net positive Capital Account balances (after such allocation and after
hypothetically increasing such Capital Account balances by amounts of any Company Minimum Gain and Member
Minimum Gain that would be allocated to the Members if all Company assets were sold for an amount equal to the
Company indebtedness encumbering such assets) were distributed to the Members in the order and priority specified
in subsections (6) of this Article,
           2. Allocation of Losses. Items of net loss and deduction shall be allocated among the Members in the manner
necessary to reduce each Member’s Capital Account to an amount equal to the amount of cash such Member would be
entitled to receive pursuant to subsections (6) of this Article if an amount of cash equal to the net positive Capital Account
balances after such allocation and after hypothetically increasing such Capital Account balances by amounts of any
Company Minimum Gain and Member Minimum Gain that would be allocated to the Members if all Company assets
were sold for an amount equal to the Company indebtedness encumbering such assets) were distributed to the Members
in the order and priority specified in subsections (6) of this Article.
        3. Other Allocations. It is the intention of the Members that allocations of profits and losses made to the
Members have economic effect equivalence in accordance with the regulations under Internal Revenue Code Section
704(b). The provisions of Section 6(1) and Section 6(2) shall be interpreted consistent with, and subject to, the terms




                                                              8

===== PAGE 167 =====
set forth on Exhibit A attached hereto, which terms shall be incorporated herein as if restated in full in this Article 6,
as applicable to the operations of the Company.
         4.    Capital Accounts. Each Member shall have a Capital Account which shall consist of its
                   (a) Capital Contributions to the capital of the Company;
                   (b) Plus, the amount of profits allocated to it pursuant to Section 6(1);
                   (c) Minus, the amount of losses associated to it pursuant to this Article 6(2); and
                   (d) Minus, all amounts distributed to or on behalf of such Member pursuant hereto;
         5. Interest on Capital Accounts. No interest shall be paid or accrued on any Member’s Capital Account
except as herein provided.
          6.   Distributions. All distributions of the Company shall be distributed to the Members in the following order
of priority:
                   a)   to the Members in accordance with and in proportion to each Member’s accrued but undistributed
                        Preferred Distribution, if any;
                   b) to the Members in accordance with and in proportion to each Member’s capital contribution in the
                      Company until each Member receives the total amount of its capital contributions;
                   c)   to the Members in accordance with the Members’ Membership Interests in the Company as
                        provided in Section 3(1).
Prior to any distribution of cash flow arising from the sale or disposition of all or substantially all of the Company’s
property, the Company shall first apply such cash flow to the payment of any unpaid expenses and outstanding loans of
the Company prior to the distributions set forth in this subsection.
                                                   ARTICLE 7
                                              TRANSFER OF INTEREST

          1. Prohibition Against Transfer. Except as hereinafter set forth, no Member may sell, assign, transfer,
encumber or otherwise dispose of all or any part of the property of the Company or its interest in the Company whether
direct or indirect through any number of tiers without the consent of the Manager which consent may be withheld in its
sole and absolute discretion. It is the intent of this section to maintain the control of the Company in the natural persons
who own the Company on the Effective Date. Any transfer not in accordance with this Article 7 shall have no effect and
will not be recognized by the Company. Upon the death of any individual who has interest in the Company on the date
that the Company initially acquires the property, such interest shall devise to his heirs.

          2. Assignment by Members. Any Member shall have the right to transfer or assign all or part of its share to
a subsidiary or affiliated company or to an entity in which the Member is the Manager or controlling party with full control
of all aspects of the entity (hereinafter a “Permitted Assignee”). Any Member or any Permitted Assignee may also transfer
or assign part, but not all, of its or their share to individuals who control that Member or any Permitted Assignee, to heir
lineal descendants (hereinafter “Family Members”), or to any or all of the foregoing; provided, however, transfers or
assignments to Family Members shall not include any voting rights, which voting rights shall be reserved by the Member
or the Permitted Assignee which is making the transfer or assignment to the Family Member.
         3. Assumption by Assignee. Any assignee or transferee who acquires all or part of a share in the Company
pursuant to the terms of this Agreement or otherwise shall take such share subject to all of the terms and conditions of this
Agreement; and such person shall not be considered to have such share until he shall agree in writing to assume the




                                                             9

===== PAGE 168 =====
obligations of his assignor or transfer hereunder and if such person is not at the time of such transfer a party to this
Agreement, to otherwise be bound by the terms and conditions of this Agreement.
                                                      ARTICLE 8
                                                     DISSOLUTION
          1. Termination or Dissolution. Upon termination of the Company, the Company shall be dissolved and its
affairs and business shall be liquidated forthwith. All property of the Company shall be sold or otherwise disposed of and
the new proceeds therefor, if any, and the remaining assets of the Company shall be applied first to satisfy the costs,
expenses, charges and liabilities of the Company, either by payment or the establishment of an adequate reserve to cover
the same and the balance shall be distributed to the Members in accordance with the distribution provisions of this
Agreement.
          2. Tax Effect of Termination or Dissolution. It is the intention of the Members that allocations made to the
Members have economic effect equivalence in accordance with the regulations under Internal Revenue Code Section
704(b). The provisions of this Section 8(2) shall be interpreted consistent with, and subject to, the terms set forth on
Exhibit A attached hereto, which terms shall be incorporated herein as if restated in full in this Article 8, as applicable
to the termination and dissolution of the Company. If a Member has a deficit balance in its Capital Account (after
giving effect to all contributions, distributions, and allocations for all tax years, including the tax year in which such
liquidation occurs), such Member shall have no obligation to make any contribution to the capital of the Company
with respect to such deficit, and such deficit shall not be considered a debt owed to the Company or to any other
Person for any purpose whatsoever.
         3. Dissolution Procedure. Within thirty (30) days of the termination of the Company or an event of
Dissolution as set forth in this Agreement, the Manager shall retain the services of a qualified appraiser to value all of the
property of the Company. The assets of the Company shall be distributed to the Members if practical and if acceptable to
all Members, or sold at prices in accordance with said appraisal.
         4. Continuation. Nothing contained herein shall be construed as limiting the ability of the remaining Members
from execution of a new Agreement and continuing the business of the Company, all in accordance with the Act and the
applicable provision of the Code.
        5. Events of Dissolution. The Company shall dissolve and commence to wind up its affairs upon the
occurrence of any of the following, in addition to those set forth elsewhere herein (each a “Dissolution Event”):
                (a) The written consent of the manager and the members holding three quarters (3/4) of all of the
         Membership Interests to dissolve;
                   (b) The entry of a decree of judicial dissolution under Section 18-802 of the Act; or
                   (c) Ninety (90) days after the date on which the Company no longer has at least one Member, unless
         at least one new Member is admitted to the Company within such 90-day period by such Member’s legal
         representative.
         The Members hereby agree that, notwithstanding any provision of the Act, the Company shall not dissolve prior
         to the occurrence of a Dissolution Event. For avoidance of doubt, any event that terminates the continued
         membership of any Member shall not cause the Company to be dissolved or its affairs to be wound up, and upon
         the occurrence of any such event, the Company shall be continued without dissolution.

                                                      ARTICLE 9
                                                   MISCELLANEOUS
         1. Property Manager. Upon agreement of the Managers, a property management company shall be hired for
the management of any real property owned by the Company. Any property management agreement shall be in writing,
and shall include a provision that the agreement may be terminated upon one full calendar month’s written notice. Any
Member may have an ownership or controlling interest in the chosen property management company. The Management
Fee paid to the Manager shall be reduced by any amount paid to a third party management company.
         2. Members Consent. The Company is not required to hold any meetings. Whenever the consent or the vote
of the Members is required in this Agreement, the Manager shall notify all of the Members of the need for consent or a
vote, which notice may be via email and a phone conversation to each Member. Such consent shall be deemed granted
and such vote shall be deemed approved upon the receipt by the Manager of the required amount of affirmative email



                                                             10

===== PAGE 169 =====
responses by Members representing the required amount of Membership Interests for such consent as set forth in this
Agreement. In the event that the percentage of the Company Interest required for a specific action is not specified in this
Agreement, such action shall be approved upon the consent of the majority of the Membership Interests.
           3. Notices. Except as may be otherwise specifically provided for in this Agreement, no notices, requests,
consent, approval, waiver or other communication under this Agreement shall be effective unless in writing and either (a)
personally served thereon or (b) sent by regular mail and by certified mail, return receipt requested, with postage prepaid,
or (c) commercial express delivery service providing delivery against receipt, addressed to the parties at the addresses on
file with the Manager, or (d) sent via email to the principal of the Member and such principal of the Member receiving the
notice sends a confirming email.. If personally served or sent via commercial delivery service, any such matter shall be
deemed given for all purposes hereunder at the time of such service or, if by mail as herein provided, at the time of
depositing same in a post office box regularly maintained by the United States Postal Service. If such notice is sent via
email then notice shall be deemed given upon the receipt of the confirming email.
         4. Indemnities. Each Member hereby indemnifies and holds harmless the Manager, the other Members, and
the Company from and against any losses, costs, damages or liabilities which said other Members or the Company, or
both, may suffer or incur as a result of the activities of the indemnifying Member, whether or not wrongful, outside the
scope of this Agreement.
          5. Headings. The headings used in this Agreement are used for administrative purposes only and do not
constitute substantive matter to be considered in construing the terms of this Agreement.
          6. Entire Agreement. This Agreement, together with the Certificate of Formation of the Company, contains
the entire understanding between the parties and supersedes any prior understandings or written or oral agreements between
them respecting the within subject matter.
         7. Parties Bound. This Agreement shall be binding upon and inure the benefit of the parties hereto and their
respective successors and assigns except as otherwise set forth herein.
         8. Gender. Wherever herein the singular number is used, the same shall include the plural, and the masculine
gender shall include the feminine and neuter genders, and vice versa, as the context shall require. This Agreement may be
executed in several counterparts, each of which shall be an original, but all of which shall constitute one and the same
instrument.
          9. New Jersey Law. The rights and remedies of the Members and Manager shall be construed in accordance
with the laws of the State of New Jersey.
          10. Construction. It is the expressed purpose of the parties hereto to have the Company, in all cases, for federal
and State income taxes, treated as a partnership. Therefore, in the event any Section of this Agreement shall be subject to
interpretation, in all events the interpretation placed upon this Agreement shall be the interpretation which provided the
most favorable tax treatment to the Company and the interpretation which will advance the purpose of having the Company
treated as a partnership.
          11. Ratification. The Members, by their execution hereof, hereby adopt and ratify all actions taken, liabilities
incurred, contracts executed, and agreements entered into prior to the date hereof by the Company via the actions of the
Manager and/or his designees.

          12. Heter Iska. The undersigned members of the Company hereby declare and admit that all advances made
between themselves with respect to capital contributions, loans to the company, capital calls, or any other mortgages,
guarantees, preferred investments, or any other advances, that would violate any of the laws of Ribbis shall be structured
as an Iska investment subject to the following terms. In exchange for the monies advanced, the recipient/beneficiary shall
transfer to the investor a share (of equal value) in his assets as an Iska. In the event that no such investment exists, or if the
recipient participates in a more lucrative investment opportunity in the future, the recipient shall assign a share of that
investment to the investor. This investment shall be owned by the investing partner, and all profits, capital gains, or losses
shall be his sole responsibility. The recipient shall have complete authority to manage this investment on behalf of the
investor, in the manner deemed most beneficial to the investor. Any investment owned by the recipient may be used to
create this Iska, provided that the venture is Halachacly permissible. This Iska may only be collected from the assets of the
Company and the investor shall have no claim against the personal assets of any of the recipients, unless otherwise specified
in an agreement between the parties. The recipient shall receive five percent of the profits as compensation for his services
managing this Iska, and shall be liable for the monies as a watchman. The recipient must verify any claim of loss through



                                                               11

===== PAGE 170 =====
the testimony of two Halachacly acceptable witnesses in an Orthodox Jewish court of law. Any claim regarding the amount
of profits generated by the joint venture must be verified with a solemn oath, administered by an Orthodox Jewish court
of law. In addition, the investor may demand full access to all legal records, documents, financial statements or receipts
that the investor deems necessary to verify the claims of the recipient. In the event of a claim of loss, the investor may
demand a full and immediate payment of the remaining balance of this Iska. It is agreed that if the recipient pays the
amount equal to the principal, interest, penalties, default rates, preferred returns, membership dilution, buyout rights, or
other benefits called for by this Agreement, as per the payment schedule of this Agreement, as payment for the investor's
share of the principal and profits of the Iska investment, the investor shall waive his right to demand verification of the
results of the investment. Any additional profits shall belong solely to the recipient. Such payment shall reflect a final
settlement, and the recipient shall have no further claim for compensation, management or services fees, unless specified
to the contrary in other agreements executed between the parties. Any payments made that are not justified by this Iska
agreement shall reduce the principal balance of this Iska. However, any future payments made to avoid providing the
abovementioned verification, shall be increased to compensate for any discrepancy. This section shall override any other
section, even those that do not reference this section. Any mention of loans or interest shall be interpreted as an Iska and
to payments mentioned above. This declaration is legally binding and may be enforced in any Jewish or civil court, even
in the event that one of the parties was unaware of its existence. This agreement has been executed in an effective manner
and with a proper Kinyan, if required.

          13. Dispute Resolution. All disputes that may arise regarding or associated with this Agreement and for which
an arbitration provision is not otherwise specified in this Agreement, shall be resolved and established exclusively by
binding arbitration by Bais Havaad of Lakewood, New Jersey. The Members may pursue any remedy allowed by the
arbitrators including commencing an action in a secular court which shall be commenced exclusively in the Courts of
Ocean County, New Jersey. Any final determination rendered by the arbitrator(s) may be confirmed and/or entered in any
court having jurisdiction thereof.

         14. Counterparts. This Agreement may be executed in counterparts which together shall constitute one fully
executed Agreement. This Agreement shall become effective upon the exchange of facsimile or emailed signatures. A
copy of any parties’ signature shall be the same as an original.

                                                  Exhibit A

                                                Tax Provisions

1.   Special Allocations. The following special allocations shall be made in the following order:

               (a)      Minimum Gain Chargeback. Except as otherwise provided in Regulations Section
     1.704-2(f), notwithstanding any other provision of this Exhibit, if there is a net decrease in Company
     Minimum Gain during any fiscal year, each Member shall be specially allocated items of Company income
     and gain for such fiscal year (and, if necessary, subsequent fiscal years) in an amount equal to such Member's
     share of the net decrease in Company Minimum Gain, determined in accordance with Regulations Section
     1.704-2(g). Allocations pursuant to the previous sentence shall be made in proportion to the respective
     amounts required to be allocated to each Member pursuant thereto. The items to be so allocated shall be
     determined in accordance with Regulations Sections 1.704-2(f)(6) and 1.704-2(j)(2). This Section 1(a) is
     intended to comply with the minimum gain chargeback requirement in Regulations Section 1.704-2(f) and
     shall be interpreted consistently therewith.

              (b)      Member Minimum Gain Chargeback. Except as otherwise provided in Regulations
     Section 1.704-2(i)(4), notwithstanding any other provision of this Exhibit, if there is a net decrease in Member
     Nonrecourse Debt Minimum Gain attributable to a Member Nonrecourse Debt during any fiscal year, each
     Member who has a share of the Member Nonrecourse Debt Minimum Gain attributable to such Member
     Nonrecourse Debt, determined in accordance with Regulations Section 1.704-2(i)(5), shall be specially
     allocated items of Company income and gain for such fiscal year (and, if necessary, subsequent fiscal years)
     in an amount equal to such Member's share of the net decrease in Member Nonrecourse Debt, determined in
     accordance with Regulations Section 1.704-2(i)(4). Allocations pursuant to the previous sentence shall be
     made in proportion to the respective amounts required to be allocated to each Member pursuant thereto. The
     items to be so allocated shall be determined in accordance with Regulations Sections 1.704-2(i)(4) and
     1.704-2(j)(2). This Section 1(b) is intended to comply with the minimum gain chargeback requirement in
     Regulations Section 1.704-2(i)(4) and shall be interpreted consistently therewith.

              (c)       Qualified Income Offset. In the event any Member unexpectedly receives any
     adjustments, allocations, or distributions described in Regulations Section 1.704-1(b)(2)(ii)(d)(4), Section
     1.704-1(b)(2)(ii)(d)(5), or Section 1.704-1(b)(2)(ii)(d)(6), items of Company income and gain shall be
     specially allocated to such Member in an amount and manner sufficient to eliminate, to the extent required
     by the Regulations, the deficit balance in the Capital Account of such Member as quickly as possible,
     provided, that an allocation pursuant to this Section 1(c) shall be made only if and to the extent that such
     Member would have an deficit balance in its Capital Account after all other allocations provided for in this
     Exhibit have been tentatively made as if this Section 1(c) were not in this Exhibit.

               (d)      Gross Income Allocation. In the event any Member has a deficit Capital Account at the
     end of any fiscal year that is in excess of the amount such Member is obligated to restore pursuant to the
     penultimate sentences of Regulations Sections 1.704-2(g)(1) and 1.704-2(i)(5), such Member shall be
     specially allocated items of Company income and gain in the amount of such excess as quickly as possible,
     provided, that an allocation pursuant to this Section 1(d) shall be made only if and to the extent that such
     Member would have a deficit Capital Account in excess of such amount after all other allocations provided
     for in this Exhibit have been made as if Section 1(c) and this Section 1(d) were not in this Agreement.

              (e)        Nonrecourse Deductions. Nonrecourse Deductions for any fiscal year shall be specially
     allocated to all of the Members in proportion to the Membership Interests of the Members.

              (f)     Member Nonrecourse Deductions. Any Member Nonrecourse Deductions for any fiscal
     year shall be specially allocated to the Member who bears the economic risk of loss with respect to the
     Member Nonrecourse Debt to which such Member Nonrecourse Deductions are attributable in accordance
     with Regulations Section 1.704-2(i)(1).




                                                       14

===== PAGE 173 =====
                   (g)     Section 754 Adjustments. To the extent an adjustment to the adjusted tax basis of any
         Company asset pursuant to Code Section 734(b) or Section 743(b) is required, pursuant to Regulations
         Section 1.704-1(b)(2)(iv)(m)(2) or Section 1.704-1(b)(2)(iv)(m)(4), to be taken into account in determining
         Capital Accounts as the result of a distribution to a Member in complete liquidation of such Member's interest
         in the Company, the amount of such adjustment to the Capital Accounts shall be treated as an item of gain
         (if the adjustment increases the basis of the asset) or as an item of loss (if the adjustment decreases such
         basis), and such gain or loss shall be specially allocated to the Members in accordance with their interests in
         the Company in the event Regulations Section 1.704-1(b)(2)(iv)(m)(2) applies, or to the Member to whom
         such distribution was made in the event Regulations Section 1.704-1(b)(2)(iv)(m)(4) applies.

          2.       Curative Allocations. The allocations set forth in Section 1 (the “Regulatory Allocations”) are
intended to comply with certain requirements of the Regulations. It is the intent of the Members that, to the extent
possible, all Regulatory Allocations shall be offset either with other Regulatory Allocations or with special allocations
of other items of Company income, gain, loss, or deduction pursuant to this Section 2. Therefore, notwithstanding any
other provision of this Exhibit (other than the Regulatory Allocations), the Manager shall make such offsetting special
allocations of Company income, gain, loss, or deduction in whatever manner it determines appropriate so that, after
such offsetting allocations are made, each Member's Capital Account balance is, to the extent possible, equal to the
Capital Account balance such Member would have had if the Regulatory Allocations were not part of this Agreement
and all Company items were allocated pursuant to Article 6(1) and Article 6(2). In exercising their discretion under
this Section 2, the Manager shall take into account future Regulatory Allocations under Sections 1(a) and 1(b) that,
although not yet made, are likely to offset other Regulatory Allocations previously made under Sections 1(e) and 1(f).

         3.        Loss Limitation. Losses allocated pursuant to Article 6(1) hereof shall not exceed the maximum
amount of losses that can be allocated without causing any Member to have an deficit balance in its Capital Account
at the end of any fiscal year. In the event some but not all of the Members would have a deficit balance in its Capital
Account as a consequence of an allocation of losses pursuant to Article 6(1) hereof, the limitation set forth in this
Section 3 shall be applied on a Member by Member basis and losses not allocable to any Member as a result of such
limitation shall be allocated to the other Members in accordance with the positive balances in such Members’ Capital
Accounts so as to allocate the maximum permissible losses to each Member under Regulations Section
1.704-1(b)(2)(ii)(d).

         4.        Other Allocation Rules.

          (a)     For purposes of determining the profits, losses, or any other items allocable to any period, profits,
losses, and any such other items shall be determined on a daily, monthly, or other basis, as determined by the Manager
using any permissible method under Code Section 706 and the Regulations thereunder.

           (b)     Solely for purposes of determining a Member's proportionate share of the "excess nonrecourse
liabilities" of the Company within the meaning of Regulations Section 1.752-3(a)(3), the Members' interests in
Company profits are in proportion to the Membership Interests of such Members.

         (c)     In the event that the Company has taxable income that is characterized as ordinary income under
the recapture provisions of the Code, each Member's distributive share of taxable gain or loss from the sale of
Company assets shall (to the extent possible) include a proportionate share of this recapture income equal to such
Member's share of prior cumulative depreciation deductions with respect to the assets that gave rise to the recapture
income.

          (d)       It is the intention of the parties that the Capital Accounts of the Members immediately before the
liquidation of the Company shall be as nearly equal as possible to the amounts that they would receive in such
liquidation if Article 6(8) governed liquidating distributions. Therefore, in the fiscal year the Company is liquidated,
should there be any difference between the Capital Accounts of the Members and the amounts they would otherwise
be entitled to receive in liquidation if Article 6(8) governed, then profits and losses in such fiscal year shall be specially
allocated among the Members so that, to the maximum extent possible, their Capital Accounts shall equal the amounts
to which they would be entitled if Article 6(8) governed liquidating distributions.




                                                             15

===== PAGE 174 =====
        5.        Tax Allocations: Code Section 704(c). In accordance with Code Section 704(c) and the
Regulations thereunder, income, gain, loss, and deduction with respect to any property contributed to the capital of
the Company shall, solely for tax purposes, be allocated among the Members so as to take account of any variation
between the adjusted basis of such property to the Company for federal income tax purposes and its initial Gross Asset
Value using the "traditional method" pursuant to the Regulations under Code Section 704(c).

         In the event the Gross Asset Value of any Company asset is adjusted pursuant to subparagraph (ii) of the
definition of “Gross Asset Value” set forth below, subsequent allocations of income, gain, loss, and deduction with
respect to such asset shall take account of any variation between the adjusted basis of such asset for federal income
tax purposes and its Gross Asset Value in the same manner as under Code Section 704(c) and the Regulations
thereunder.

         Any elections or other decisions relating to such allocations shall be made by the Manager in any manner
that reasonably reflects the purpose and intention of this Agreement. Allocations pursuant to this Section 5 are solely
for purposes of federal, state, and local taxes and shall not affect, or in any way be taken into account in computing,
any Member's Capital Account or share of profits, losses, other items, or distributions pursuant to any provision of
this Agreement.

For purposes of this Exhibit A, the following definitions shall apply:

         (i)      “Company Minimum Gain” has the same meaning as is given to the term “partnership minimum
gain” in Sections 1.704-2(b)(2) and 1.704-2(d) of the Regulations.

         (ii)     “Company Nonrecourse Debt” has the same meaning as is given to the term “partner nonrecourse
debt” in Section 1.704-2(b)(4) of the Regulations.

         (iii)    “Company Nonrecourse Debt Minimum Gain” means an amount, with respect to each Member
Nonrecourse Debt, equal to the Company Minimum Gain that would result if such Member Nonrecourse Debt were
treated as a Nonrecourse Liability, determined in accordance with Section 1.704-2(i)(3) of the Regulations.

        (iv)    “Company Nonrecourse Deductions” has the same meaning as is given to the term “partner
nonrecourse deductions” in Sections 1.704-2(i)(1) and 1.704-2(i)(2) of the Regulations.

         (v)      “Gross Asset Value” means, with respect to any asset, the asset's adjusted basis for federal income
tax purposes, except as follows:

                   (i) The initial Gross Asset Value of any asset contributed by a Member to the Company shall be the
         gross fair market value of such asset, as determined by the contributing Member and the Manager.

                  (ii) The Gross Asset Values of all Company assets shall be adjusted to equal their respective gross
         fair market values, as determined by the Manager, as of the following times: (a) the acquisition of an
         additional interest in the Company by any new or existing Member in exchange for more than a de minimis
         Capital Contribution; (b) the distribution by the Company to any Member of more than a de minimis amount
         of property as consideration for an interest in the Company; and (c) the liquidation of the Company within
         the meaning of Regulations Section 1.704-1(b)(2)(ii)(g); provided, however, that adjustments pursuant to
         clauses (a) and (b) above shall be made only if the Manager reasonably determines that such adjustments are
         necessary or appropriate to reflect the relative economic interests of the Members in the Company.

                  (iii) The Gross Asset Value of any Company asset distributed to any Member shall be adjusted to
         equal the gross fair market value of such asset on the date of distribution as determined by the distributee and
         the Manager.

                  (iv)     The Gross Asset Values of Company assets shall be increased (or decreased) to reflect any
         adjustments to the adjusted basis of such assets pursuant to Code Section 734(b) or Section 743(b), but only
         to the extent that such adjustments are taken into account in determining Capital Accounts pursuant to
         Regulations Section 1.704-1(b)(2)(iv)(m) and subparagraph (vi) of the definition of “Profits” and “Losses”



                                                           16

===== PAGE 175 =====
         set forth in this definition of “Gross Asset Value”, or pursuant to Section 1(e) hereof; provided, however,
         that Gross Asset Values shall not be adjusted pursuant to this subparagraph (iv) to the extent that an
         adjustment pursuant to subparagraph (ii) of this definition is required in connection with a transaction that
         would otherwise result in an adjustment pursuant to this subparagraph (iv).

If the Gross Asset Value of an asset has been determined or adjusted pursuant to subparagraph (i), subparagraph (ii), or
subparagraph (iv) of this definition, such Gross Asset Value shall thereafter be adjusted by the depreciation taken into
account with respect to such asset for purposes of computing profits and losses.

         (vi)     “Nonrecourse Deductions” has the meaning set forth in Section 1.704-2(b)(1) of the Regulations.

         (vii)    “Nonrecourse Liability” has the meaning set forth in Section 1.704-2(b)(3) of the Regulations.

                            AMENDED AND RESTATED OPERATING AGREEMENT
                                               OF
                                   LINCOLNWOOD OPERATOR LLC

          THIS AMENDED AND RESTATED OPERATING AGREEMENT (the “Agreement”) is made and entered
into as of January 1, 2021 by QUINTO DELTA LLC (“Quinto”), UKR CONSULTING LLC and SKILLED VENTURE
LLC (“Venture”) (individually “Member” and collectively “Members”).
          In consideration of the mutual covenants herein contained, the Members hereby enter into this Amended and
Restated Operating Agreement to control the operations of Lincolnwood Operator LLC , a limited liability company
formed under and pursuant to the laws of the State of Rhode Island (hereinafter sometimes referred to as the “Company”)
for the purposes and upon the terms, provisions and conditions hereinafter set forth.
        Unless the context shall clearly indicate otherwise, all words and phrases used in this Amended and Restated
Operating Agreement shall have the meaning set forth in the Rhode Island Limited Liability Company Act, (the “Act”).
                                                       ARTICLE 1
                                                      FORMATION
         1.   Name. The name of the Company shall be Lincolnwood Operator LLC.
        2. Place of Business. The principal place of business of the Company shall be 1608 Route 88, Suite 301,
Brick, New Jersey or such other place or places as the Manager may from time to time designate.
          3. Formation; Ratification. The Company was formed by the filing of the Certificate of Formation on May
15, 2020. The Members do hereby ratify and authorize any and all actions taken in the name of the Company since the
date of formation and acknowledge that this Agreement shall control the operations of the Company from the date of its
formation.
          4. Member’s Authority. Except as otherwise expressly and specifically provided for in this Agreement or in
the Certificate of Formation, no Member shall have any authority to act for, or assume any obligations or responsibility on
behalf of, the Company or any other Member of the Company.
         5. Member’s Prior Obligations. No Member shall be responsible or liable for any obligation or debt of any
other Member incurred before the formation of the Company except as may be set forth herein, and each party indemnifies
and agrees to hold all others harmless from such obligations and indebtedness.
        6. Other Business. Nothing herein shall restrict any Member from conducting any other business or activity
whatsoever (including any operation or business or activity which competes with the business of the Company).
        7. Registered Agent. The registered agent for the Company shall be Registered Agents Inc, with an address
of 47 Wood Avenue, Suite 2, Barrington, RI 02806.
         8. Term. The Company shall continue until the earliest to occur of the sale or other disposition of all or
substantially all of the property of the Company, the incompetence or permanent disability of the sole remaining Member
or upon the approval of the Manager and three-quarters (3/4) of all of the Membership Interests, at least coextensive with
the remaining term of the Mortgage.
          9. Purposes of the Company. The purpose of the Company shall be to engage in any business in which a
limited liability company may lawfully engage in the State of Rhode Island.
          10. Powers of the Company. The Company shall at any time and from time to time have the power and
authority to do or cause to be done all things necessary or desirable to carry out the purposes of the Company described in
Section 1(9) hereof, including, but not limited to acquiring or disposing of real or personal (or mixed) property or any
interest therein and manage property for others, obtaining financing for the Company, including refinancing and further
including interim and permanent financing (and refinancing) for the acquisition and development of real property and
financing the operations of the Company, sell or transfer or mortgage or place (or suffer to be placed) an encumbrance on
any property of the Company, enter into leases or other similar arrangements, construct, reconstruct, alter, maintain, repair
and demolish improvements on or to any real property of the Company, enter into contracts with others to perform or carry

out any or all of the foregoing and to do all other things necessary or desirable to conduct the business of the Company
and to carry out the terms and conditions of this Agreement.

the Company, and that all ownership interests in the property of the Company shall be vested in the Company. No Member
shall have any entitlement to the distribution of any property other than cash earned by the Company and shall not have

as a Member of the Company.

until the date which is five (5) years from the date that Venture makes its full required capital contribution to the Company
and upon such member not participating as a partner or investor in any other endeavor related to the ownership or operation
of nursing homes or related businesses without the participation of Quinto. In the event that the abovementioned member
is not employed full time by the Company by the abovementioned date or does not comply with its other covenants set
forth herein, Quinto shall have the right to acquire all of Venture’s membership interests for the amount that Venture has
contributed to the Company minus all distributions which Venture has already received. The assignment of Venture’s
interests shall become effective automatically upon the delivery to Venture of said amount. To the extent that Venture has
already received the distributions in the amount set forth herein, the assignment shall become effective automatically
without any further action from any of the parties. Venture hereby agrees that in the event that it is no longer entitled to
its Membership Interests as set forth herein, it authorizes the Majority Member to execute any assignments required to
effectuate this Section.

       8. Additional Members. The Company may admit additional members and issue Membership Interests to
